- [6.9.3 Password hashing and HMAC key handling](#693-password-hashing-and-hmac-key-handling)

**Implemented controls:** Alchemy API key handling for WebSocket RPC, Embedded SQLite store access control and data-at-rest protection, Password hashing and HMAC key handling.

**Assessment:** Secrets live in source: the JWT private key (🟠 [F-019](#f-019)), the security-answer HMAC key (🟡 [F-149](#f-149)) and a wallet mnemonic (🟡 [F-142](#f-142)). Passwords use unsalted `MD5` (🟠 [F-043](#f-043) (User passwords stored as unsalted `MD5` hashes without a strength policy)), and card numbers and TOTP secrets are stored unencrypted (🟡 [F-121](#f-121), 🟡 [F-122](#f-122), 🟡 [F-148](#f-148)).

**Findings in this category without a control:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code
- 🟡 [F-121](#f-121) — Card numbers and TOTP secrets stored unencrypted
- 🟡 [F-122](#f-122) — TOTP secrets stored unencrypted in the Users table
- 🟡 [F-135](#f-135) — Access log files served to unauthenticated callers
- 🟡 [F-142](#f-142) — Hardcoded Ethereum wallet mnemonic exposes private key
- 🟡 [F-144](#f-144) — Confidential records stored in an unencrypted SQLite file
- 🟡 [F-145](#f-145) — Testing account credentials hardcoded in frontend bundle
- 🟡 [F-146](#f-146) — Plaintext user account passwords embedded in client
- 🟡 [F-148](#f-148) — TOTP secrets are stored in plaintext in the user table
- 🟡 [F-149](#f-149) — Security answer HMAC key is hardcoded in source

<a id="alchemy-api-key-handling-for-websocket-rpc"></a>
#### 6.9.1 Alchemy API key handling for WebSocket RPC

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - the key comes from the environment over `wss`, but fails open and sits in the URL path.

<!-- Model implementation note (facts for the intro): routes/nftMint.ts:18; routes/web3Wallet.ts:20 -->
`routes/nftMint.ts` and `routes/web3Wallet.ts` read the Alchemy key from the environment and connect over `wss`.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The API key is read from the environment, not hard-coded, and the connection uses wss. An empty-string fallback means the code does not fail closed on a missing key. The key sits in the URL path, which can leak through logs. The error logging shown logs only the error message. -->
Two weaknesses remain:

- An empty-string fallback means a missing key does not fail closed.
- The key sits in the URL path, which can leak through logs. Error logging shows only the message.

A hardcoded wallet mnemonic in the same surface is covered by 🟡 [F-142](#f-142) — Hardcoded Ethereum wallet mnemonic exposes private key.

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="embedded-sqlite-store-access-control-and-data-at-rest-protection"></a>
#### 6.9.2 Embedded SQLite store access control and data-at-rest protection

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - a plain file with no encryption at rest or database authentication.

<!-- Model implementation note (facts for the intro): models/index.ts:33-43 createSequelize; models/index.ts:75 initModels -->
Sequelize opens `data/juiceshop.sqlite` through `createSequelize()` in `models/index.ts`. The file holds data classified Confidential.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Sequelize uses the SQLite dialect with placeholder credentials ('username'/'password'), which the embedded engine ignores. The database is a plain file at data/juiceshop.sqlite. In the inspected file there is no encryption at rest and no database-level authentication, so protection depends on filesystem permissions and process isolation. Those were not inspected here. The file holds data classified Confidential. Sequelize ORM access, lines 30-43, 75, retries SQLITE_BUSY up to 5 times and uses IMMEDIATE transactions, which helps write consistency and availability under contention. -->
Protection rests on file permissions and process isolation, which were not inspected:

- The SQLite file is unencrypted (🟡 [F-144](#f-144)).
- Card numbers and TOTP secrets are stored as plain columns (🟡 [F-121](#f-121), 🟡 [F-120](#f-120), 🟡 [F-122](#f-122)).
- Retry and IMMEDIATE transactions help write consistency, not confidentiality.

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="password-hashing-and-hmac-key-handling"></a>
#### 6.9.3 Password hashing and HMAC key handling

**Status:** 🟠 Weak - unsalted `MD5`, plus hardcoded HMAC and signing keys.

`lib/insecurity.ts` hashes passwords with an `MD5` `hash()` and signs security answers with an HMAC keyed by a literal in the same file.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Passwords are hashed with unsalted MD5. The HMAC key used for security answers is hardcoded in source. The JWT private key is also embedded in source (value redacted at line 21). -->
Three weaknesses stack here:

- Passwords are one fast, unsalted hash (🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — User passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes without a strength policy (User passwords stored as unsalted `MD5` hashes without a strength policy), 🟠 [F-053](#f-053) — Passwords hashed with unsalted MD5 (Passwords hashed with unsalted `MD5`)).
- The HMAC key for security answers is hardcoded (🟡 [F-149](#f-149)).
- The JWT private key is embedded in source (🟠 [F-019](#f-019)).

**Relevant findings**

- None linked to this control; see the other findings of this category.

<a id="file-upload-constraints"></a>
#### 6.10.2 File upload constraints

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - size and MIME limits exist, but the MIME type is client-declared and content is not verified.

Memory-storage uploads are capped at 200 KB. Disk uploads check the MIME type against an allowlist and sanitize the filename, deriving the extension from the MIME type.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): Memory-storage uploads are limited to 200 KB. Disk uploads check the MIME type against an allowlist (client-declared) and sanitize the filename, deriving the extension from the MIME type. Constraints stop short of content checks:

- Magic bytes are not verified in the inspected code.
- The memory photo upload has no size limit and trusts the client MIME (🟡 [F-156](#f-156)).
- Zip entries are written without path or size limits (🟠 [F-030](#f-030), 🟡 [F-153](#f-153)).
- XML parsing is unbounded (🟡 [F-152](#f-152)).

### 6.11 Operations Runtime and Supply Chain Controls

**Build path.** [Figure 1b](#figure-1b) shows the inputs, build systems and release artifacts that Juice Shop evidences.

Highlighted path of 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Dependency lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration — .npmrc:1: npm registry → GitHub Actions → docker.io/bkimminich/juice-shop. The path stops where the evidence ends.

**Attack entries:**

- **Manipulated dependency**
  - 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Dependency lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration
  - 🟡 [F-101](#f-101) — Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled
- **Manipulated CI input**
  - 🟡 [F-100](#f-100) — Heroku CLI installed by piping a remote script to sh in deploy job
  - 🟡 [F-102](#f-102) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tag or branch
  - 🟡 [F-103](#f-103) — Container base images referenced by floating tags without digest
- **Replaced release artifact**
  - 🟡 [F-099](#f-099) — Missing Container Image Signing

**Not attributable to a build step:**

- 🟡 [F-159](#f-159) — Container Runs as Root


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-026](#w-026), [W-027](#w-027), [W-051](#w-051)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing - no lockfile, no install integrity and floating base images. CodeQL and the container runtime are the strengths.

<!-- Model implementation note (facts for the intro): .npmrc:1; release.yml:38-40,55; Dockerfile:4-5,19; ci.yml:36,51 -->
`.npmrc` sets `package-lock=false`, so CI, release and Docker builds run `npm install`. Global tools use semver ranges.

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): package-lock=false disables lockfile generation, so CI, release and Docker builds use npm install with floating versions. <a id="workflow-token-permissions-and-secret-scoping"></a>
#### 6.11.3 Workflow token permissions and secret scoping

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - CodeQL declares least privilege, but CI and release show no permissions block.

<!-- Model implementation note (facts for the intro): codeql-analysis.yml:11-14; ci.yml:153; release.yml:74-77 -->

Token scope is uneven:

- The inspected parts of `ci.yml` and `release.yml` have no top-level `permissions`, so `GITHUB_TOKEN` gets the repository default (🟡 [F-160](#f-160)).
- A tag-triggered release job uses Docker Hub secrets.
- A release tag name is interpolated into a shell step (🟡 [F-098](#f-098)).

`ci.yml` was read only to line 200, and other workflows were not read.

The diagram shows the intended webhook notification path:
```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    participant App as Juice Shop Server
    participant Hook as Configured Webhook Endpoint
    App->>App: Read webhook URL from environment
    App->>Hook: POST challenge details
    Hook-->>App: Response
```

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The webhook URL comes from an operator environment variable, not request input. The call is sent without protection:

- It carries no authentication or signature and has no timeout.
- It sends the CTF flag and host details.
- The full webhook URL is written to the log.
- Transport protection depends on the configured URL scheme.

#### 6.12.1 WebSocket connection authentication

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - the connection handler accepts every socket with no authentication or identity check.

This control is not evidenced in the repository.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The connection handler accepts every socket without authentication or identity check and immediately replays all stored notifications (including CTF flags) to the new client. Each handler also performs no per-event authorization. -->
Three gaps open the channel:

- Every new socket receives all stored notifications, including flags (🟡 [F-143](#f-143)).
- Events alter challenge state and broadcast flags without authentication (🟡 [F-105](#f-105) — Unauthenticated Socket.IO events alter challenge state and broadcast flags (Unauthenticated Socket\.IO events alter challenge state and broadcast flags)).
- Handlers do no per-event authorization, and clients can assert challenge solves (🟡 [F-091](#f-091)).

<a id="chat-stream-handling"></a>
#### 6.12.2 Chat stream handling

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - the stream is parsed client-side, a malformed chunk throws, and tool execution was not inspected.

The client posts the full message history to `/rest/chat` and parses the server-sent event stream. Tool-call deltas are passed through to the caller.

**Security assessment**

<!-- Model assessment (facts the narrative must not contradict): The client sends the full message history to /rest/chat and parses the SSE stream. JSON.parse is not guarded, so a malformed chunk throws. Tool-call deltas are passed through to the caller. How tool calls are executed or approved was not inspected, so no rating is given for that. -->
The stream handling has four weak points:

- `JSON.parse` is unguarded, so a malformed chunk throws.
- The chat endpoint is unauthenticated and its LLM usage is unbounded (🟡 [F-154](#f-154)).
- Caller-supplied history and roles reach the model unvalidated (🟡 [F-115](#f-115)).
- Tool execution and approval were not inspected, so no rating is given.

**Relevant findings**

- None.

### 6.13 Defense-in-Depth Summary

<!-- §6.13 FORMAT — NEVER a table, at most 120 words, no file:line. Two short bullet lists: **What holds** (the individual positive controls that exist, strongest first, e.g. distroless runtime image, RS256 algorithm choice) and **Repair first** (the control-boundary repairs that would restore layered defense, each with its mitigation link [M-NNN](#m-nnn)). Do NOT emit a Markdown table — `| header |` lines under §6.13 are a contract violation. -->

**What holds**

- Distroless, non-root runtime image.
- CodeQL security-extended scanning and an SBOM.
- Most workflow actions pinned to commit SHAs.
- `RS256` token signing with a 6-hour expiry.
- `sanitize-html` on notification text.

**Repair first**

- Parameterize the login and search queries.
- Move signing and HMAC keys out of source.
- Add server-side ownership and role checks.
- Enable lockfiles and pin install inputs.
- Authenticate `Socket.IO` connections.

<a id="w-001"></a>
### W-001 — Database access relies on concatenated queries

🔴 Critical · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Database queries are assembled from application values instead of passing those values through an enforced parameterised data-access path. This leaves every call site responsible for preserving query structure.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Raw SQL string interpolation.** Queries are built by concatenating request values across several routes, so each route needs its own fix rather than one patch.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — SQL injection in product search query
- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — SQL injection in login query enables authentication bypass

**Practice sites:**

- `routes/login.ts:34`
- `routes/search.ts:23`

**Architecture evidence:** Parameterized Queries, ORM / Repository Layer, Source evidence (`routes/vulnCodeSnippet.ts:46`)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), [C-09](#c-09)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — provide one parameterised repository or query-builder path and prohibit application-value interpolation in database queries
- **Tactical** — ● [M-168](#m-168) — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria, ● [M-170](#m-170) — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

<a id="w-002"></a>
### W-002 — Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

🔴 Critical · implementation weakness · confirmed · 4 findings

Model binding accepts caller-selected attributes across a privileged write boundary. The affected binding path does not separate fields callers may change from fields reserved for server-side policy.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Mass-assignment / unscoped object binding.** Generated resource routes bind whole request bodies to models, including the role field, with no enforced write allowlist.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration

<a id="w-003"></a>
### W-003 — Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

🟠 High · implementation weakness · confirmed · 9 findings

Token validation is implemented in application code rather than behind one vetted and consistently configured authentication path. Algorithm, issuer, audience, and key-handling checks can diverge between callers.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature
- 🟡 [F-082](#f-082) — Unauthenticated review creation

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — OAuth login trusts userinfo profile without checking
- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — JWT verification does not pin the signing algorithm
- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — JWT verification does not pin the expected signing
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist
- 🟡 [F-083](#f-083) — Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature
- 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm
- 🟡 [F-099](#f-099) — Missing Container Image Signing

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02), [C-03](#c-03), [C-08](#c-08), [C-09](#c-09)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — centralise token validation on a vetted library with pinned algorithms, issuer and audience checks, and managed signing keys
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-173](#m-173) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat user identity, ◑ [M-228](#m-228) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims, ◕ [M-175](#m-175) — Validate token audience server-side before OAuth login, ● [M-179](#m-179) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call, ◕ [M-180](#m-180) — Pin RS256 in all JWT verification calls, ◑ [M-154](#m-154) — Manual review: verify JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist, ◕ [M-181](#m-181) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (F-025), ◑ [M-160](#m-160) — Manual review: verify Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature verification, ◑ [M-229](#m-229) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat user id, ◑ [M-148](#m-148) — Sign container images and verify signatures before use

<a id="w-004"></a>
### W-004 — Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

🟠 High · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Request handlers do not validate input against one enforced server-side schema or allowlist at the boundary - validation is either absent on the vulnerable parameters or limited to rejecting selected bad patterns (a blacklist). New encodings and unanticipated input forms can therefore reach downstream parsers or interpreters.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Data erasure layout parameter allows local file read
- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Zip Slip lets uploaded zip entries be written

**Architecture evidence:** Schema Validation, Allowlist Validation, Source evidence (`server.ts:204`), Source evidence (`server.ts:246`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/app/photo-wall/photo-wall.component.ts:93`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/hacking-instructor/helpers/helpers.ts:262`) (+4 more)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce server-side schemas and domain-specific allowlists before input reaches parsing, persistence, or command construction
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-185](#m-185) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (F-029), ◕ [M-186](#m-186) — Contain extracted entries to the target directory

<a id="w-007"></a>
### W-007 — Application data crosses into executable code

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 3 findings

An interpreter evaluates application values as code. The affected path mixes data processing with executable program construction instead of constraining callers to predefined operations.

**Practice sites:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Server-side eval of stored username on profile render
- 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Stored username evaluated as JavaScript on profile
- 🟡 [F-161](#f-161) — B2B order lines evaluated by notevil inside node vm

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — replace evaluation of application values with a data parser or a fixed set of operations whose arguments remain data
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-153](#m-153) — Manual review: verify Server-side eval of stored username on profile render, ● [M-171](#m-171) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-010), ◕ [M-190](#m-190) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-036), ◑ [M-299](#m-299) — Parse order lines as JSON instead of evaluating

<a id="w-008"></a>
### W-008 — Document-database queries take their structure from application values

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 3 findings

Document-store queries take operators or JavaScript predicates from application values instead of fixed query structures with typed values. Callers can change what a query selects, for example through injected operators or evaluated expressions.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Route id interpolated into MarsDB \$where predicates
- 🟠 [F-055](#f-055) — NoSQL \$where injection in order tracking
- 🟡 [F-157](#f-157) — Attacker-controlled \$where JavaScript blocks

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — build document queries from fixed operator structures, coerce request values to the expected scalar types, and disable server-side JavaScript evaluation in the database
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-188](#m-188) — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where, ◑ [M-158](#m-158) — Manual review: verify NoSQL \$where injection in order tracking, ◕ [M-207](#m-207) — Query orders by equality selector instead of \$where, ◑ [M-165](#m-165) — Manual review: verify Attacker-controlled \$where JavaScript blocks event loop, ◑ [M-297](#m-297) — Replace \$where with numeric equality selector

<a id="w-009"></a>
### W-009 — OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

The client starts OAuth or OpenID Connect sign-in without a per-request state value that its callback verifies. A forged authorization response can then complete sign-in in the victim's browser with the attacker's grant.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Google sign-in uses implicit flow without state

**Affected components:** [C-08](#c-08)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — start every sign-in through a maintained OAuth/OIDC client that generates and verifies state (and PKCE) for each authorization request
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-176](#m-176) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (F-017)

<a id="w-010"></a>
### W-010 — Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

🟠 High · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Browser code retains reusable authentication credentials in script-readable storage. Script execution in the same origin can obtain the credentials and reuse the authenticated session.

**Architectural anti-pattern - SPA without BFF.** The browser frontend keeps the session token in storage readable by scripts and no backend-for-frontend holds it server-side, so any script injection hands over the session. Moving the token to a protected cookie is a design change.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-056](#f-056) — Bearer and 2FA tokens stored in localStorage and a script-readable cookie

**Affected components:** [C-08](#c-08)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move reusable credentials behind a server-managed session boundary and keep browser-visible state free of reusable authentication secrets
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-208](#m-208) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

<a id="w-011"></a>
### W-011 — Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

🟠 High · design weakness · design-risk · 5 findings

Cryptographic keys, credentials, and other high-entropy secrets are embedded as literals in source or config rather than resolved at runtime from a managed secret store, so anyone with repository read access obtains reusable signing material and credentials.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Secrets hardcoded in source.** The token signing key is committed to the repository instead of a managed secret store, so reading the source is enough to forge identities.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code
- 🟡 [F-142](#f-142) — Hardcoded Ethereum wallet mnemonic exposes private key
- 🟡 [F-145](#f-145) — Testing account credentials hardcoded in frontend bundle
- 🟡 [F-146](#f-146) — Plaintext user account passwords embedded in client
- 🟡 [F-149](#f-149) — Security answer HMAC key is hardcoded in source
- `server.ts:289`
- `models/securityAnswer.ts:45`

**Architecture evidence:** Managed Secret Store, Runtime Secret Injection, Source evidence (`routes/checkKeys.ts:10`), Source evidence (`lib/insecurity.ts:21`)

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01), [C-03](#c-03), [C-04](#c-04), [C-08](#c-08), [C-09](#c-09)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move every secret to a managed secret store or injected environment configuration, rotate the exposed values, and add secret-scanning to CI
- **Tactical** — ● [M-178](#m-178) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store, ◑ [M-283](#m-283) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (F-142), ◑ [M-286](#m-286) — Remove embedded test credentials from client code, ◑ [M-287](#m-287) — Remove real credentials from frontend tutorial code, ◑ [M-289](#m-289) — Move the HMAC key to external secret storage

### W-012 — Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

🟡 Medium · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Sensitive API routes and real-time channels are exposed without an enforced authentication check at the endpoint boundary. Access control depends on each handler (or the caller) remembering to require a session, so an unauthenticated client can reach privileged operations directly.

- 🟡 [F-105](#f-105) — Unauthenticated Socket\.IO events alter challenge state and broadcast flags
- 🟡 [F-143](#f-143) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), [C-06](#c-06)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authentication centrally at the routing and channel boundary so every exposed endpoint requires a verified session unless explicitly marked public
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-246](#m-246) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (F-105), ◑ [M-284](#m-284) — Authenticate socket connections before emitting flags

<a id="w-028"></a>
### W-028 — Sensitive cookies remain accessible to browser scripts

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Sensitive cookies are issued without HttpOnly protection. Browser scripts in the same origin can read values that should remain confined to the browser's HTTP cookie handling.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-150](#f-150) — Session token cookie lacks HttpOnly and Secure flags

**Affected components:** [C-09](#c-09)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — issue sensitive cookies server-side with HttpOnly protection and keep script-readable application preferences separate from session credentials
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-290](#m-290) — Set secure session-cookie attributes

<a id="w-050"></a>
### W-050 — Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

🟡 Medium · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 2 findings

Password, token, or integrity protection uses a weak hash, predictable random source, or insufficient work factor. The application may use a standard library, but the selected primitive does not provide the required security property.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — User passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes without a strength policy
- 🟠 [F-053](#f-053) — Passwords hashed with unsalted MD5

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01), [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — standardise on a password KDF, a CSPRNG for secrets, and modern authenticated cryptographic primitives with centrally reviewed parameters
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-197](#m-197) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (F-043), ◕ [M-205](#m-205) — Hash passwords with bcrypt or argon2id

<a id="w-051"></a>
### W-051 — Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

🟡 Medium · design weakness · design-risk · 2 findings

CI/CD workflows resolve third-party actions and other build dependencies to mutable tags or branches instead of immutable commit digests, so a retagged or compromised upstream runs inside the pipeline with its token and secret scope.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Dependency lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration — .npmrc:1
- 🟡 [F-102](#f-102) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tag or branch

**Architecture evidence:** Commit-SHA Action Pinning, Dependency Provenance Verification, Source evidence (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:42`), Source evidence (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:188`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — pin every third-party action and build dependency to an immutable commit SHA (or a vetted internal mirror) and enforce SHA-pinning in CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-183](#m-183) — Commit lockfiles and install from them, ◕ [M-244](#m-244) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

### 🔴 Critical (5)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · SQL injection in product search query

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Validation: The search route accepts unvalidated query input at the browser ingress and forwards it to a SQL sink.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends GET `/rest/products/search?q=`')) `UNION SELECT ... FROM Users`--.

At `routes/search.ts:23` the q parameter is interpolated directly into a raw SQL string that runs on the application database. Interpolating request-controlled text into SQL in general also allows modifying arbitrary rows or escalating to full database control.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  20 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  21 │     let criteria: any = req.query.q === 'undefined' ? '' : req.query.q ?? ''
  22 │     criteria = (criteria.length <= 200) ? criteria : criteria.substring(0, 200)
→ 23 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Products WHERE ((name LIKE '%${criteria}%' OR description LIKE '%${criteria}%') AND deletedAt IS NULL) ORDER BY name`) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line unionSqlInjectionChallenge dbSchemaChallenge
  24 │       .then(([products]: any) => {
  25 │         const dataString = JSON.stringify(products)
  26 │         if (challengeUtils.notSolved(challenges.unionSqlInjectionChallenge)) { // vuln-code-snippet hide-start
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-168](#m-168) — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01) · Rules: INJ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-sql-injection-in-product-search-query)

<a id="t-008"></a><a id="f-008"></a>
#### F-008 · Insecure Direct Object Reference

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Contradicted by verification:** a control was evidenced at [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) at `routes/payment.ts:21`; [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) at `routes/payment.ts:41`; [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) at `routes/payment.ts:70`. Check this finding before acting on it.

**Reliability:** Low. This may be a false positive: a review or check contradicts it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (3):** `routes/payment.ts`: 21, 41, 70

**Issue:** A signed-in attacker puts another user's ID into the owner field of the request (`req.body.UserId` and the like); the query trusts it instead of the session identity, so the attacker reads or changes every other user's records - horizontal privilege escalation across all accounts.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
routes/payment.ts
  18 │ export function getPaymentMethods () {
  19 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  20 │     const displayableCards: displayCard[] = []
→ 21 │     const cards = await CardModel.findAll({ where: { UserId: req.body.UserId } })
  22 │     cards.forEach(card => {
  23 │       const displayableCard: displayCard = {
  24 │         UserId: card.UserId,
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-152](#m-152) — Manual review: verify Insecure Direct Object Reference (Card) · ● [M-169](#m-169) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🔴 [F-008](#f-008))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-insecure-direct-object-reference-card-in-payment)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · Server-side eval of stored username on profile render

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Stored username reaches eval with no validation, but the branch runs only if `usernameXssChallenge` is enabled; flag state is not shown in served source.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** An authenticated user sets their username to #{`<javascript>`} via POST `/profile`, then requests GET `/profile`. When the username matches /#{(.*)}/ and the `usernameXssChallenge` is enabled (a feature flag, not shown off by default), the inner text is passed to eval, executing arbitrary JavaScript in the Node process.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  58 │         if (!code) {
  59 │           throw new Error('Username is null')
  60 │         }
→ 61 │         username = eval(code) // eslint-disable-line no-eval
  62 │       } catch (err) {
  63 │         username = '\\' + username
  64 │       }
```

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ◑ [M-153](#m-153) — Manual review: verify Server-side eval of stored username on profile render · ● [M-171](#m-171) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🔴 [F-010](#f-010))

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-server-side-eval-of-stored-username-on-profile-render)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>
#### F-011 · Mass assignment of admin role during user registration

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical - reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint; verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-004 (Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration), AC-B-002 (User gains more rights than intended); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with email, password, `passwordRepeat` and role set to admin. The registration pre-handlers at `server.ts:408-422` only trim fields and record challenge solves; `registerAdminChallenge` detects exactly this body.

None of them strips role, `deluxeToken` or `isActive`. The attacker logs in, receives a JWT whose role is admin, and uses administrative functions of the shop, including access to other users' data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  498 │   ]
  499 │
  500 │   for (const { name, exclude, model, include } of autoModels) {
→ 501 │     const resource = finale.resource({
  502 │       model,
  503 │       endpoints: [`/api/${name}s`, `/api/${name}s/:id`],
  504 │       excludeAttributes: exclude,
```

**Fix:** ● [M-172](#m-172) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-mass-assignment-of-admin-role-during-user-registration)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · SQL injection in login query enables authentication bypass

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Validation: The validation leg assumes login bodies are never concatenated into SQL; the login handler at this crossing does exactly that.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/rest/user/login` with email set to "admin@juice-`sh.op`'--" or "' OR 1=1--" and any password. At `routes/login.ts:34` the email is interpolated into raw SQL.

The injected quote closes the string and the comment removes the password and `deletedAt` conditions, so the query returns the admin row or the first user row. UNION-based payloads can also read arbitrary tables, and interpolating request text into SQL in general allows modifying rows or escalating to full database control.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  31 │
  32 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  33 │     verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 34 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  35 │       .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  36 │         const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
  37 │         if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-170](#m-170) — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-01](#tc-input-01) · Rules: INJ-001 · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.4](#34-sql-injection-in-login-query-enables-authentication-bypass)

### 🟠 High (59)

<a id="t-019"></a><a id="f-019"></a>
#### F-019 · JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code

**Severity:** 🟠 High - secret committed to the public source repo - extractable on clone, no prior access needed; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases) _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02), [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The `RS256` private key used to sign JWTs is a literal. Anyone who can read the repository or a deployed bundle obtains it and can sign arbitrary tokens, for example ones claiming the admin role or another user's id. A hardcoded HMAC key used for security answers is also present on line 42.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A signing key is embedded as a literal constant in source.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ● [M-178](#m-178) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02), [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-jwt-rsa-private-signing-key-embedded-in-source-code)

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-001 (User reads or changes data of another user or tenant); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (7)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Authorization: The `/rest/basket/:id` object id is not checked against the authenticated user at this handler.

**Instances (7):** `routes/basket.ts`: 19; `routes/updateProductReviews.ts`: 17; `routes/order.ts`: 35; `server.ts`: 426, 603, 604, 605

**Issue:** Several endpoints require a valid JWT but look up objects only by an id from the request and never check that the caller owns them. Any logged-in user can therefore read or change other customers' data. The finding holds 7 places, so it covers further places beyond the six locations cited here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
server.ts
  424 │   app.use('/b2b/v2', security.isAuthorized())
  425 │   /* Check if the quantity is available in stock and limit per user not exceeded, then add item to basket */
→ 426 │   app.put('/api/BasketItems/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemUpdate()))
  427 │   app.post('/api/BasketItems', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.quantityCheckBeforeBasketItemAddition()), utils.asyncHandler(basketItems.addBasketItem()))
  428 │   /* Accounting users are allowed to check and update quantities */
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-167](#m-167) — Scope basket query to authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-301

<a id="t-013"></a><a id="f-013"></a>
#### F-013 · Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** `getUserId` calls `security.decode` (`jws.decode`, no verification) on the bearer token. An attacker forges an unsigned token with `data.id` of a victim; `getOrderById` then loads the victim's email and returns orders whose masked email matches, and `getUserNameFromToken` places the victim's username in the system prompt.

`/rest/chat` has no `isAuthorized` guard.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  43 │   const token = utils.jwtFrom(req)
  44 │   if (!token) return undefined
→ 45 │   const decoded = security.decode(token) as { data?: { id?: number } } | undefined
  46 │   return decoded?.data?.id
  47 │ }
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-173](#m-173) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat user identity

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

The attacker then logs in with P and takes over the account permanently.

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-174](#m-174) — Require and verify the current password on change

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

An access token issued to any other Google client for a victim (for example, one collected by a malicious third-party app) therefore signs the attacker in as the victim.

The source read does not show whether `routes/login.ts` treats `oauth:true` logins differently.

  32 │           next: () => {
```

The handler checks only that the card belongs to the user and then calls `WalletModel.increment` with `req.body.balance`.

No charge is made, and the amount has no type, sign or range check, so the user mints store credit.

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · Data erasure layout parameter allows local file read

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`

**Found by:** scanners and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** A logged-in user posts to `/dataerasure` with layout set to an arbitrary server path. At `routes/dataErasure.ts:104`, `req.body` is spread into the `res.render` options, so the request-controlled path is used as the layout file and its first 100 characters are returned.

A request-controlled path with ../ can read arbitrary files such as `/etc/passwd`, source code or secrets, or reach locations outside the intended root.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  102 │
  103 │       if (req.body.layout) {
→ 104 │         const filePath: string = path.resolve(req.body.layout).toLowerCase()
  105 │         const isForbiddenFile: boolean = (filePath.includes('ftp') || filePath.includes('ctf.key') || filePath.includes('encryptionkeys'))
  106 │         if (!isForbiddenFile) {
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-185](#m-185) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (🟠 [F-029](#f-029))

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01) · Rules: INJ-004

<a id="t-030"></a><a id="f-030"></a>
#### F-030 · Zip Slip lets uploaded zip entries be written

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:34`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01), [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads a .zip to `/file-upload` with an entry named, for example, ../..`/ftp/legal.md`. At `routes/fileUpload.ts:34` the only check is that the resolved path includes the application root or working directory (`absolutePath.includes`, line 33).

A 200 KB upload limit applies.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  32 │     challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.fileWriteChallenge, () => { return absolutePath === path.resolve('ftp/legal.md') })
  33 │     if (absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))) {
→ 34 │       await pipeline(entry.stream(), fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName))
  35 │     }
  36 │   }
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-186](#m-186) — Contain extracted entries to the target directory

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01), [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-031"></a><a id="f-031"></a>
#### F-031 · IDOR

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:68`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Line 68 is a lookup used for stock checking; the item is not returned, and the finale update/read handlers that would expose or modify it are unseen.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Any authenticated user can substitute another user's resource ID in the URL and read or modify the target record because the query filter has no ownership clause. Admin-only routes are excluded via the counter-pattern allowlist.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  66 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  67 │     try {
→ 68 │       const item = await BasketItemModel.findOne({ where: { id: req.params.id } })
  69 │       const user = security.authenticatedUsers.from(req)
  70 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.basketManipulateChallenge, () => { return user && req.body.BasketId && user.bid != req.body.BasketId }) // eslint-disable-line eqeqeq
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-155](#m-155) — Manual review: verify IDOR resource lookup by URL parameter without ownership filter… · ◕ [M-187](#m-187) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟠 [F-031](#f-031))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-002

<a id="t-034"></a><a id="f-034"></a>
#### F-034 · Route id interpolated into MarsDB \$where predicates

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Document-database queries take their structure from application values

**Instances (2):** `routes/showProductReviews.ts`: 36; `routes/trackOrder.ts`: 18

**Issue:** Request input read reaches an executable \$where expression. When `noSqlCommandChallenge` is disabled, the id is coerced with `Number()`. When that challenge is disabled, non-word characters are stripped.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
routes/showProductReviews.ts
  34 │     const t0 = new Date().getTime()
  35 │
→ 36 │     db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id }).then((reviews: Review[]) => {
  37 │       const t1 = new Date().getTime()
  38 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlCommandChallenge, () => { return (t1 - t0) > 2000 })
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◕ [M-188](#m-188) — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02) · Rules: INJ-NODE-006

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · NoSQL operator injection in review update and like selectors

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) - Juice Shop Express API → marsdb Embedded Store · Query construction: Request JSON object reaches the reviews collection selector without scalar validation, violating the selector assumption.

**Instances (2):** `routes/likeProductReviews.ts`: 25; `routes/updateProductReviews.ts`: 18

**Issue:** Both review handlers use `req.body.id` as the _id selector without a type check. They sit behind the `isAuthorized()` JWT middleware at `server.ts:634` (`lib/insecurity.ts:52`). The JSON body parser configuration was not fully read.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
routes/updateProductReviews.ts
  16 │     const user = security.authenticatedUsers.from(req) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line forgedReviewChallenge
  17 │     db.reviewsCollection.update( // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line forgedReviewChallenge
→ 18 │       { _id: req.body.id }, // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line noSqlReviewsChallenge forgedReviewChallenge
  19 │       { $set: { message: req.body.message } },
  20 │       { multi: true } // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line noSqlReviewsChallenge
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◕ [M-189](#m-189) — Require scalar string review id and single update

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-02](#tc-input-02)

<a id="t-036"></a><a id="f-036"></a>
#### F-036 · Username spliced into Pug template source before

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-019](#w-019) - Application data crosses into template source

**Issue:** `getUserProfile` reads the stored username, a persisted record read, under the condition if (username). It string-replaces _username_ in the Pug template source with that value, or with the eval result, and calls `pug.compile`.

A third says the unsafe path is observed but attacker control, access prerequisites and the active configuration require verification.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  85 │     try {
  86 │       const pug = (await import('pug')).default
→ 87 │       const fn = pug.compile(template)
  88 │       const CSP = `img-src 'self' ${user?.profileImage}; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'`
  89 │
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-190](#m-190) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🟠 [F-036](#f-036))

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03) · Rules: INJ-NODE-008

<a id="t-043"></a><a id="f-043"></a>
#### F-043 · User passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes without a strength policy

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey, scanners and verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-050](#w-050) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Instances (2):** `models/user.ts`: 76; `lib/insecurity.ts`: 41

**Issue:** Every User password goes through the model setter, which stores `security.hash(clearTextPassword)`. `lib/insecurity.ts:41` implements that as an unsalted `MD5` digest, a fast hash with no salt and no work factor.

One part also notes that the password field has no validate rule, so the model enforces no minimum strength. If the stored hashes leak, most user passwords can be recovered offline, including admin passwords.

This leads to account takeover and to credential-stuffing attacks on other sites.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `user.ts:76` calls `security.hash(clearTextPassword)`. `insecurity.ts:41` defines hash as `MD5` hex with no salt or iterations. The password field at `user.ts:73-74` declares no validation.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-197](#m-197) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (🟠 [F-043](#f-043))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04), [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02), [TC-CRYPTO-01](#tc-crypto-01) · Rules: CRYPTO-001

<a id="t-044"></a><a id="f-044"></a>
#### F-044 · Local file read

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/dataErasure.ts:107`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A logged-in user POSTs `/dataerasure` with body field layout set to an arbitrary server path (e.g. ../..`/config` or `/etc/passwd`). The whole `req.body` is spread into `res.render` options, so the view engine loads that file as the layout; only paths containing 'ftp', '`ctf.key`' or 'encryptionkeys' are denied, and the first 100 chars of the rendered file are returned.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  105 │         const isForbiddenFile: boolean = (filePath.includes('ftp') || filePath.includes('ctf.key') || filePath.includes('encryptionkeys'))
  106 │         if (!isForbiddenFile) {
→ 107 │           res.render('dataErasureResult', {
  108 │             ...req.body,
  109 │             ...themeVars
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-198](#m-198) — Stop spreading request body into render options

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-73](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/73.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-045"></a><a id="f-045"></a>
#### F-045 · Unauthenticated memories listing serializes full

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/memory.ts:24`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-029](#w-029) - Unauthenticated memories listing serializes full associated User records

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker calls GET `/rest/memories`; each memory includes its full User model instance, exposing email, role, password hash, `deluxeToken` and `totpSecret` of posting users unless a model scope excludes them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `MemoryModel.findAll({ include: [UserModel] })` returned whole via `res.json` (line 25); User model defines password, `deluxeToken`, `totpSecret`.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-199](#m-199) — Limit included User attributes in the memories response

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-046"></a><a id="f-046"></a>
#### F-046 · Memories listing exposes full user records including

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/memory.ts:24`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Route 630 has no auth and includes UserModel, but the UserModel definition (attributes, scopes) is not supplied, so returned secret fields are unproven.

**Weakness:** [W-038](#w-038) - Memories listing exposes full user records including secrets (`memory.ts:24`)

**Issue:** GET `/rest/memories` has no authentication and runs `MemoryModel.findAll({include:[UserModel]})`; UserModel has no attribute exclusion, so each memory's owner email, `MD5` password hash, `totpSecret` and `deluxeToken` are returned.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

→ 24 │     const memories = await MemoryModel.findAll({ include: [UserModel] })
  25 │     res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: memories })
  26 │   }
```

**Fix:** ◑ [M-156](#m-156) — Manual review: verify Memories listing exposes full user records including secrets · ◕ [M-200](#m-200) — Restrict included user attributes

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-359](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/359.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-047"></a><a id="f-047"></a>
#### F-047 · User directory and deluxe tokens exposed to any authenticated

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:484`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Any registered customer calls GET `/api/Users` or GET `/api/Users/:id`. These routes are guarded only by `isAuthorized` (`server.ts:363`, 365).

The finale User resource excludes only password and `totpSecret`, so the response returns every user's email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `profileImage`, with no ownership or admin check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

  485 │     { name: 'Product', exclude: [], model: ProductModel },
  486 │     { name: 'Feedback', exclude: [], model: FeedbackModel },
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-201](#m-201) — Restrict user listing to admins and exclude sensitive fields

<a id="t-048"></a><a id="f-048"></a>

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:277`
