<a id="t-049"></a><a id="f-049"></a>
#### F-049 · Passwords sent in GET query to change-password and exposed in access logs

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Instances (2):** `routes/changePassword.ts`: 15; `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts`: 54

**Issue:** The client `changePassword` method at `frontend/src/app/Services/user.service.ts:54` sends the current, new and repeat passwords as GET query parameters to `/rest/user/change-password`, and the server route reads them from the query string. These URLs are recorded in server and proxy access logs and in monitoring tools, so anyone with log access learns users' current and new passwords.

The only limit noted is that logs are kept for 2 days.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
routes/changePassword.ts
  13 │   return async ({ query, headers, connection }: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  14 │     const currentPassword = query.current as string
→ 15 │     const newPassword = query.new as string
  16 │     const newPasswordInString = newPassword?.toString()
  17 │     const repeatPassword = query.repeat
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-203](#m-203) — Accept passwords only in POST request bodies

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07), [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-050"></a><a id="f-050"></a>
#### F-050 · XML external entity resolution on complaint upload

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: XXE parse and 400-char echo are shown, but `/file-upload` route registration, auth and the challenge-enabled gate are not in supplied source.

**Weakness:** [W-021](#w-021) - XML external entity resolution on complaint upload (`xml.ts:35`)

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads an .xml file to `/file-upload` with a DOCTYPE referencing `file:///etc/passwd`. `parseXmlString` enables NOENT and DTDLOAD with filesystem input providers, and the parsed result is echoed in the error message (first 400 chars).

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  33 │ export async function parseXmlString (data: string, timeoutMs = 2000): Promise<string> {
  34 │   const libxml2 = await loadLibxml2()
→ 35 │   const option = libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOENT | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOBLANKS | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOCDATA
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◑ [M-157](#m-157) — Manual review: verify XML external entity resolution on complaint upload · ◕ [M-204](#m-204) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-051"></a><a id="f-051"></a>
#### F-051 · XML parser enables external entities with host filesystem access

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:35`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-022](#w-022) - XML parser enables external entities with host filesystem access (`xml.ts:35`)

**Issue:** `parseXmlString` parses with entity substitution and DTD loading enabled and registers filesystem input providers, so an XML document declaring an external entity to `file:///etc/passwd` is resolved and returned in the serialized output. The upload route that calls it was not read in this job.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  33 │ export async function parseXmlString (data: string, timeoutMs = 2000): Promise<string> {
  34 │   const libxml2 = await loadLibxml2()
→ 35 │   const option = libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOENT | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_DTDLOAD | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOBLANKS | libxml2.ParseOption.XML_PARSE_NOCDATA
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◕ [M-204](#m-204) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-052"></a><a id="f-052"></a>
#### F-052 · XML upload parsed with external entities and DTD loading

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `lib/xml.ts:38`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-06](#tc-input-06))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads an .xml file to `/file-upload` that declares an external entity such as `file:///etc/passwd`. libxml2 resolves it through the registered filesystem providers, and the expanded content is returned in the error message.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  36 │   const sandbox = { libxml2, data, option }
  37 │   vm.createContext(sandbox)
→ 38 │   const xmlDoc = vm.runInContext('libxml2.XmlDocument.fromString(data, { option })', sandbox, { timeout: timeoutMs })
  39 │   const xmlString = xmlDoc.toString()
  40 │   xmlDoc.dispose()
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ◕ [M-204](#m-204) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-06](#tc-input-06)

<a id="t-053"></a><a id="f-053"></a>
#### F-053 · Passwords hashed with unsalted MD5

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-050](#w-050) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** Passwords are hashed with `security.hash`, which is unsalted `MD5`, and change-password compares `MD5` digests. An attacker who obtains the Users table can quickly recover most passwords with rainbow tables or GPU cracking.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/changePassword.ts:39` compares `security.hash(currentPassword)` with the stored password. `lib/insecurity.ts:41` implements hash as `MD5` hex, and the User model setter stores passwords with it.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-205](#m-205) — Hash passwords with bcrypt or argon2id

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-054"></a><a id="f-054"></a>
#### F-054 · Server-side request forgery

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-05](#tc-input-05))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-7](#tb-7) - Juice Shop Express API → external · Egress destination: Outbound fetch destination crossing the network boundary is fully requester-controlled.

**Issue:** Any logged-in user can POST `imageUrl` to `/profile/image/url`, for example `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` or an internal service URL. The handler reads it at line 19, and `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24` calls `fetch(url)` with no scheme, host or IP allow-list.

The only control is a cookie-session login check (line 21).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  22 │       if (loggedInUser) {
  23 │         try {
→ 24 │           const response = await fetch(url)
  25 │           if (!response.ok || !response.body) {
  26 │             throw new Error('url returned a non-OK status code or an empty body')
```

**Fix:** Validate the URL scheme + host against an explicit allow-list before issuing outbound requests → ◕ [M-206](#m-206) — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Classification:** Server-Side Request Forgery · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-05](#tc-input-05)

<a id="t-055"></a><a id="f-055"></a>
#### F-055 · NoSQL \$where injection in order tracking

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Injection path and unauthenticated route are shown, but exploitation depends on `reflectedXssChallenge` being enabled; that flag's state is not evidenced.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Document-database queries take their structure from application values

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) - Juice Shop Express API → marsdb Embedded Store · Query construction: A raw path parameter becomes part of an executable MarsDB \$where selector on the orders collection.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/rest/track-order/`' || true || ' . When `reflectedXssChallenge` is enabled the id is only truncated to 60 characters and concatenated into a quoted \$where JavaScript predicate, so the predicate matches every order and the server returns all customers' orders.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  16 │
  17 │     challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.reflectedXssChallenge, () => { return utils.contains(id, '<iframe src="javascript:alert(`xss`)">') })
→ 18 │     db.ordersCollection.find({ $where: `this.orderId === '${id}'` }).then((order: any) => {
  19 │       const result = utils.queryResultToJson(order)
  20 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlOrdersChallenge, () => { return result.data.length > 1 })
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◑ [M-158](#m-158) — Manual review: verify NoSQL \$where injection in order tracking · ◕ [M-207](#m-207) — Query orders by equality selector instead of \$where

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-056"></a><a id="f-056"></a>
#### F-056 · Bearer and 2FA tokens stored in localStorage and a script-readable cookie

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

**Instances (2):** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts`: 101; `frontend/src/app/two-factor-auth-enter/two-factor-auth-enter.component.ts`: 53

**Issue:** After login, the client writes the session token to `localStorage` and to a cookie that JavaScript sets, so the cookie cannot be HttpOnly. It also keeps `totp_tmp_token` in `localStorage`. The finding also holds a further place beyond the cited location (`location_count` 2).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:101` calls `localStorage.setItem('token')`. Line 104 puts the token in a cookie via `cookieService`. `request.interceptor.ts:16` reads it for the `Authorization` header.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-208](#m-208) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-057"></a><a id="f-057"></a>
#### F-057 · Password reset response leaks TOTP secret

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker who guesses or researches a victim's security answer calls the unauthenticated POST `/rest/user/reset-password`. The response contains the full updated user, including `totpSecret`. The attacker can then generate valid TOTP codes and bypass the victim's second factor.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  44 │           const updatedUser = await user.update({ password: newPassword })
  45 │           verifySecurityAnswerChallenges(updatedUser, answer)
→ 46 │           res.json({ user: updatedUser })
  47 │         }
  48 │       } else {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-209](#m-209) — Return a status only from password reset

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-058"></a><a id="f-058"></a>
#### F-058 · Authentication details route exposes all user records to any

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:601`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** Any registered customer calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details` with their own JWT. They receive every user's email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp`, profile image and login time, because the handler spreads `user.dataValues` for all users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `retrieveUserList` runs `UserModel.findAll()` and returns ...`user.dataValues` to any authenticated caller. There is no admin role check.

```typescript
  599 │   app.get('/rest/user/security-question', utils.asyncHandler(securityQuestion()))
  600 │   app.get('/rest/user/whoami', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveLoggedInUser()))
→ 601 │   app.get('/rest/user/authentication-details', utils.asyncHandler(authenticatedUsers()))
  602 │   app.get('/rest/products/search', utils.asyncHandler(searchProducts()))
  603 │   app.get('/rest/basket/:id', utils.asyncHandler(retrieveBasket()))
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-210](#m-210) — Restrict user listing to admins and allowlist fields

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-059"></a><a id="f-059"></a>
#### F-059 · Model-selected coupon tool enforces discount policy only

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-7](#tb-7) - Juice Shop Express API → external · Response trust: Model output selects the discount argument that is executed without treating the provider response as untrusted.

**Issue:** LLM06/ASI02 - Excessive Agency: an anonymous caller of POST `/rest/chat` persuades the model (LLM01/ASI01) to call `generateCoupon` with discount 99. The zod schema accepts any number and execute passes it straight to `security.generateCoupon`; the 10% maximum and verified-order conditions exist only in the system prompt.

The returned code is accepted by `discountFromCoupon` when applied to a basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  182 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 10) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
  183 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 50) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 184 │           const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  185 │           return { couponCode, discount } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  186 │         }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-211](#m-211) — Enforce coupon eligibility and cap in tool code

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-060"></a><a id="f-060"></a>
#### F-060 · Stored username evaluated as JavaScript on profile

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-006 (Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**Found by:** scanners and verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** A persisted user record read reaches `eval()`. A logged-in attacker updates their profile username to the form #{`<js>`}, for example #{`require('child_process')`...}. The only control is a cookie-token login check (line 34), and nothing restricts the evaluated code.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  59 │           throw new Error('Username is null')
  60 │         }
→ 61 │         username = eval(code) // eslint-disable-line no-eval
  62 │       } catch (err) {
  63 │         username = '\\' + username
```

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ◕ [M-190](#m-190) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🟠 [F-036](#f-036))

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03) · Rules: INJ-NODE-007

<a id="t-061"></a><a id="f-061"></a>
#### F-061 · Any authenticated user can create catalog products

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:369`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A customer sends POST `/api/Products` with any name, description and price. The generated finale create inserts the product into the shop catalog, because the route checks only that a valid token is present and no admin role.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:369` registers only `isAuthorized()`, and `insecurity.ts:52` shows it is a JWT signature check with no role. `server.ts:485` exposes ProductModel through finale. The PUT `/api/Products/:id` guard at line 370 is commented out.

```typescript
  367 │     .delete(security.denyAll())
  368 │   /* Products: Only GET is allowed in order to view products */ // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line changeProductChallenge
→ 369 │   app.post('/api/Products', security.isAuthorized()) // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line changeProductChallenge
  370 │   // app.put('/api/Products/:id', security.isAuthorized()) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line changeProductChallenge
  371 │   app.delete('/api/Products/:id', security.denyAll())
```

**Fix:** Add explicit server-side authorisation checks on every protected route → ◕ [M-212](#m-212) — Enforce server-side authorization

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-062"></a><a id="f-062"></a>
#### F-062 · Any authenticated user can read any user record by id

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:365`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Authorization: The finding's evidence line is the interface this boundary cites; CWE-639 breaks its authorization condition.

**Issue:** A logged-in customer requests GET `/api/Users/``&lt;n&gt;` for arbitrary ids. Only `isAuthorized()` runs before the finale read. The resource excludes only password and `totpSecret`, so the response discloses other users' email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp` and `profileImage`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  363 │   app.get('/api/Users', security.isAuthorized())
  364 │   app.route('/api/Users/:id')
→ 365 │     .get(security.isAuthorized())
  366 │     .put(security.denyAll())
  367 │     .delete(security.denyAll())
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-213](#m-213) — Bind user record reads to the authenticated caller

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-063"></a><a id="f-063"></a>
#### F-063 · Unauthenticated read of any recycle record by parsed ID

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:388`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** Without logging in, an attacker sends GET `/api/Recycles/1`, /2 and so on, or a JSON array such as GET `/api/Recycles/[1,2,3]`. The route has no authentication middleware. The GET `/api/Recycles` list is blocked (`server.ts:385`) and PUT and DELETE use `denyAll`, but GET by id has no control.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:388` registers `getRecycleItem` with no guard. `routes/recycles.ts:12-14` runs `findAll` filtered only on id: `JSON.parse(req.params.id)`, with no UserId condition.

```typescript
  386 │   app.post('/api/Recycles', security.isAuthorized())
  387 │   /* Challenge evaluation before finale takes over */
→ 388 │   app.get('/api/Recycles/:id', recycles.getRecycleItem())
  389 │   app.put('/api/Recycles/:id', security.denyAll())
  390 │   app.delete('/api/Recycles/:id', security.denyAll())
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-214](#m-214) — Bind recycle lookups to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-067"></a><a id="f-067"></a>
#### F-067 · Deluxe membership granted without payment

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` only charges when `paymentMode` is 'wallet' or validates a card when 'card'; any other value (or a wallet-mode user without a wallet) falls through to `user.update({role: deluxe})` and a new deluxe token is issued.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  41 │
  42 │       try {
→ 43 │         const updatedUser = await user.update({ role: security.roles.deluxe, deluxeToken: security.deluxeToken(user.email) })
  44 │         challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.freeDeluxeChallenge, () => {
  45 │           return security.verify(utils.jwtFrom(req)) && req.body.paymentMode !== 'wallet' && req.body.paymentMode !== 'card'
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-215](#m-215) — Enforce successful payment before role upgrade

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-068"></a><a id="f-068"></a>
#### F-068 · Sensitive Routes Registered Without Authentication Middleware

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (5)

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Route lacks route-level auth middleware, but the handler (`routes/profileImageFileUpload.ts`) reportedly checks the cookie and its source was not supplied.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (5):** `server.ts`: 310, 311, 598, 641, 667

**Issue:** The route registration carries no authentication marker, so an anonymous attacker may call this state-changing operation on a sensitive resource directly, unless a downstream check the scanner cannot see stops it.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
server.ts
  308 │   /* File Upload */
  309 │   app.post('/file-upload', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), checkUploadSize, checkFileType, handleZipFileUpload, handleXmlUpload, handleYamlUpload)
→ 310 │   app.post('/profile/image/file', uploadToMemory.single('file'), ensureFileIsPassed, metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageFileUpload()))
  311 │   app.post('/profile/image/url', uploadToMemory.single('file'), utils.asyncHandler(profileImageUrlUpload()))
  312 │   app.post('/rest/memories', uploadToDisk.single('image'), ensureFileIsPassed, security.appendUserId(), metrics.observeFileUploadMetricsMiddleware(), utils.asyncHandler(addMemory()))
```

**Fix:** ◑ [M-159](#m-159) — Manual review: verify Sensitive Routes Registered Without Authentication Middleware · ◕ [M-216](#m-216) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (🟠 [F-068](#f-068))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-01](#tc-authn-01) · Rules: AUTHZ-008

<a id="t-069"></a><a id="f-069"></a>
#### F-069 · Any authenticated user can list all user accounts

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-B-007 (User misuses a privileged function for another purpose); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:363`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A customer sends GET `/api/Users`. The generated finale list returns every user record except password and `totpSecret`, including e-mail addresses and roles, because the route checks only authentication.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:363` applies only `isAuthorized()` to GET `/api/Users`. `server.ts:484` excludes only password and `totpSecret`, and line 503 generates the list endpoint with no role check or owner filter.

```typescript
  361 │   app.use('/api/Feedbacks/:id', security.isAuthorized())
  362 │   /* Users: Only POST is allowed in order to register a new user */
→ 363 │   app.get('/api/Users', security.isAuthorized())
  364 │   app.route('/api/Users/:id')
  365 │     .get(security.isAuthorized())
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-217](#m-217) — Require admin role for the user list endpoint

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-071"></a><a id="f-071"></a>
#### F-071 · Product catalog writable without role authorization

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:485`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** The finale Product resource exposes create and update endpoints. PUT `/api/Products/:id` has no guard because its registration is commented out, so any anonymous client can rewrite product names, prices and descriptions. No admin role check exists on either path.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  483 │   const autoModels = [
  484 │     { name: 'User', exclude: ['password', 'totpSecret'], model: UserModel },
→ 485 │     { name: 'Product', exclude: [], model: ProductModel },
  486 │     { name: 'Feedback', exclude: [], model: FeedbackModel },
  487 │     { name: 'BasketItem', exclude: [], model: BasketItemModel },
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-218](#m-218) — Require admin role for product create and update

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-072"></a><a id="f-072"></a>
#### F-072 · Unauthenticated callers can modify any product including its

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Authorization: The ingress assumption that state-changing routes sit behind `isAuthorized` fails for the generated product update route.

**Issue:** The PUT `/api/Products/:id` route that finale generates has no guard; its `isAuthorized` registration is commented out, while POST and DELETE are guarded. An unauthenticated attacker sends a PUT with a JSON body that sets price, `deluxePrice`, name, description or image of any product, changing the catalog and checkout prices for every customer.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `finale.resource` at `server.ts:501` exposes `/api/Products/:id` with update. The guard for PUT at `server.ts:370` is commented out, and `models/product.ts:58-59` shows price fields that the body can set.

```typescript
  499 │
  500 │   for (const { name, exclude, model, include } of autoModels) {
→ 501 │     const resource = finale.resource({
  502 │       model,
  503 │       endpoints: [`/api/${name}s`, `/api/${name}s/:id`],
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-219](#m-219) — Restrict product updates to an admin role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-073"></a><a id="f-073"></a>
#### F-073 · Chatbot generateCoupon tool issues arbitrary discounts

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain contributor in AC-T-007 (Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-7](#tb-7) - Juice Shop Express API → external · Response trust: Model output (tool arguments) from the LLM provider is trusted as the coupon discount without validation.

**Issue:** POST `/rest/chat` has no auth middleware or route guard (`server.ts:638`). It passes the caller's messages to the model together with the `generateCoupon` tool. This is excessive agency (LLM06/ASI02) combined with prompt injection (LLM01).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  182 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 10) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
  183 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 50) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 184 │           const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  185 │           return { couponCode, discount } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  186 │         }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-220](#m-220) — Enforce coupon limits and eligibility in tool code

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

#### F-074 · Deluxe role granted without verified payment

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `deluxe.ts` lines 24 and 34 charge only for wallet or card; otherwise execution falls through to line 43 which sets role deluxe.

```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-221](#m-221) — Reject deluxe upgrades without a successful payment

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

#### F-075 · User registration accepts privileged role from request body

  410 │       if (req.body.email.length !== 0 && req.body.password.length !== 0) {
```

**Fix:** ● [M-222](#m-222) — Strip privileged fields from user registration

<a id="t-076"></a><a id="f-076"></a>
#### F-076 · Self-registration accepts a client-supplied admin role

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical: reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:420`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with `{"email":...,"password":...,"role":"admin"}`. The finale-generated create binds the whole body to UserModel. The role validator allows 'admin', and no middleware removes the field, so the attacker gets an administrator account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  418 │     next()
  419 │   })
→ 420 │   app.post('/api/Users', verify.registerAdminChallenge())
  421 │   app.post('/api/Users', verify.passwordRepeatChallenge()) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
  422 │   app.post('/api/Users', verify.emptyUserRegistration())
```

**Fix:** ● [M-222](#m-222) — Strip privileged fields from user registration

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-077"></a><a id="f-077"></a>
#### F-077 · Unauthenticated security answer creation for arbitrary user IDs

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical: reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:501`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** POST `/api/SecurityAnswers` has no guard; only the GET list and /:id are denied (`server.ts:395-396`). The finale create handler binds the whole body, including UserId, without `appendUserId`.

An attacker creates a security answer for any user ID that does not yet have one, then could use the security-question password reset to take over that account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:492` registers SecurityAnswer with `finale.resource` at `server.ts:501`, and only GET routes are guarded at `server.ts:395-396`. `models/securityAnswer.ts:29` shows a writable UserId. The password-reset consumer was not read.

```typescript
  499 │
  500 │   for (const { name, exclude, model, include } of autoModels) {
→ 501 │     const resource = finale.resource({
  502 │       model,
  503 │       endpoints: [`/api/${name}s`, `/api/${name}s/:id`],
```

**Fix:** ● [M-223](#m-223) — Bind security answer creation to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03), [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-078"></a><a id="f-078"></a>
#### F-078 · Prompt injection makes chatbot issue coupons with arbitrary

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-007 (Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AI-01](#tc-ai-01), [TC-AI-03](#tc-ai-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated or regular caller POSTs `/rest/chat` with crafted messages, such as forged system or assistant turns or instructions overriding the coupon policy. The model calls `generateCoupon` with discount 50 to 100.

execute passes the value directly to `security.generateCoupon` and returns a valid code, which can then be applied at checkout for a steep or full discount.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:638` registers `/rest/chat` with no guard. `chat.ts:191`/206 forwards raw `req.body.messages`, line 179 accepts an unbounded `z.number()`, and line 184 issues the coupon. The 10% policy exists only in prompt text (lines 98-103).

```typescript
  182 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 10) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
  183 │           challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge, () => discount >= 50) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 184 │           const couponCode = security.generateCoupon(discount) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  185 │           return { couponCode, discount } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge
  186 │         }
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-224](#m-224) — Enforce coupon policy server-side

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AI-01](#tc-ai-01), [TC-AI-03](#tc-ai-03)

<a id="t-079"></a><a id="f-079"></a>
#### F-079 · Address update by id without ownership check

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:450`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer sends PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;`victimAddressId`&gt; with any address fields. `appendUserId` only overwrites UserId, then the finale-generated update handler loads the Address by :id and applies the body.

Nothing compares the stored owner with the caller, so the attacker rewrites the victim's address and moves it onto their own account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  448 │   app.post('/api/Addresss', security.appendUserId())
  449 │   app.get('/api/Addresss', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.getAddress()))
→ 450 │   app.put('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId())
  451 │   app.delete('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.delAddressById()))
  452 │   app.get('/api/Addresss/:id', security.appendUserId(), utils.asyncHandler(address.getAddressById()))
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-225](#m-225) — Restrict address updates to the owner

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-022"></a><a id="f-022"></a>
#### F-022 · JWT verification does not pin the signing algorithm

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-2](#tb-2) 🌐 External - external → Authentication &amp; Session Surface · Authentication: The finding's evidence line is the interface this boundary cites; CWE-347 breaks its authentication condition.

**Issue:** An attacker forges a token whose header names a different algorithm (e.g. `HS256` keyed with the public RSA key, or none). The verifiers at lines 52, 55 and 189 pass no algorithm allowlist, so the token header selects the algorithm.

A forged token then sets the identity or role trusted by `isAuthorized`, `isAccounting` and the `tokenMap` used by `appendUserId`. A forged token can impersonate any user, or carry the accounting role, on every route that trusts these verifiers.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `expressJwt({secret: publicKey})` at 52, `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` at 55 and `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, cb)` at 189 all omit an algorithm allowlist. Whether the installed library versions honour `HS256` or none in the header was not read.

```typescript
  50 │ }
  51 │
→ 52 │ export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)
  53 │ export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
  54 │ export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ● [M-179](#m-179) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02)

<a id="t-024"></a><a id="f-024"></a>
#### F-024 · JWT verification does not pin the expected signing

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** `verify()` calls `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` without an algorithm, and `isAuthorized()` configures `express-jwt` with only a secret. Depending on library version, an attacker can craft a token with an unsigned or `HS256` header keyed with the public key, passing `isAccounting()`, `isDeluxe()` and the 2FA `tmpToken` check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  53 │ export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
  54 │ export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
→ 55 │ export const verify = (token: string) => token ? (jws.verify as ((token: string, secret: string) => boolean))(token, publicKey) : false
  56 │ export const decode = (token: string) => { return jws.decode(token)?.payload }
  57 │
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-180](#m-180) — Pin RS256 in all JWT verification calls

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Severity:** 🟠 High _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:189`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `jwt.verify` lacks algorithms, but with a PEM public key recent jsonwebtoken restricts to asymmetric algs; library version is not in the window.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** Without an explicit algorithm allowlist, attackers can forge tokens with `alg:none` (older lib versions) or use the public key as an HMAC secret to mint valid signatures.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  187 │   const token = req.cookies.token || utils.jwtFrom(req)
  188 │   if (token && authenticatedUsers.get(token) === undefined) {
→ 189 │     jwt.verify(token, publicKey, (err: Error | null, decoded: any) => {
  190 │       if (err === null && decoded?.data !== undefined) {
  191 │         authenticatedUsers.put(token, decoded)
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-154](#m-154) — Manual review: verify JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist · ◕ [M-181](#m-181) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (🟠 [F-025](#f-025))

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02) · Rules: AUTHZ-005

No role check runs, so any customer can enumerate all accounts and administrators.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  10 │ async function retrieveUserList (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {
  11 │   try {
→ 12 │     const users = await UserModel.findAll()
  13 │
  14 │     res.json({
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-226](#m-226) — Require the admin role for the user listing

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-017"></a><a id="f-017"></a>
#### F-017 · Google sign-in uses implicit flow without state

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06), [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-8](#tb-8) - Angular Frontend → external · Response trust: No state value binds the authorization request to its response, so the token returned from Google cannot be trusted as belonging to this login attempt.

**Issue:** `googleLogin` starts Google OAuth with `response_type=token` and sends no state or nonce. The callback route's `oauthMatcher` accepts any URL containing #`access_token`= and OAuthComponent logs the browser in.

The only control is that the redirect URI is matched against the configured `authorizedRedirects` list (`login.component.ts:80`).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - A state-changing request can be triggered cross-origin without a synchroniser token.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◕ [M-176](#m-176) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (🟠 [F-017](#f-017))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-06](#tc-authn-06), [TC-AUTHN-07](#tc-authn-07)

<a id="t-037"></a><a id="f-037"></a>
#### F-037 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Instances (2):** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts`: 119; `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts`: 91

**Issue:** An attacker submits feedback through POST `/api/Feedbacks` with an HTML or script payload in the comment. The About page fetches all feedback, puts each comment into an HTML string, marks it trusted with the DomSanitizer bypass, and renders it through [`innerHTML`].

The finding holds further places beyond the location cited here.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `about.component.ts:116-119` puts feedbacks[i].comment into HTML and marks it trusted. `about.component.html:51` binds it with [`innerHTML`]. Server-side feedback sanitization was not read.

```typescript
frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts
  117 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  118 │           }</p><div class="feedback-stars">(${this.stars[feedbacks[i].rating]})</div></figcaption>`
→ 119 │           feedbacks[i].comment = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(
  120 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  121 │           )
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-191](#m-191) — Remove sanitizer bypass and render comments as text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-038"></a><a id="f-038"></a>
#### F-038 · Stored XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker registers through POST `/api/Users` with an email containing HTML or script. Registration only trims the email (`server.ts:411`), and no part inspected server-side validation.

When an administrator opens the administration page, `findAllUsers` inserts `user.email` into an HTML string, marks it trusted, and renders it through [`innerHTML`] (html lines 26 and 113). Script runs in an administrator session, enabling theft of the admin token stored in `localStorage`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `administration.component.ts:73` builds `<span ...>${user.email}</span>` and marks it as trusted HTML, and the template binds it via [`innerHTML`] at `administration.component.html:26`.

```typescript
  71 │         this.userDataSourceHidden = users
  72 │         for (const user of this.userDataSource) {
→ 73 │           user.email = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(`<span class="${this.doesUserHaveAnActiveSession(user) ? 'confirmation' : 'error'}">${user.email}</span>`)
  74 │         }
  75 │         this.userDataSource = new MatTableDataSource(this.userDataSource)
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-192](#m-192) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟠 [F-038](#f-038))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-039"></a><a id="f-039"></a>
#### F-039 · DOM-based XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.html:11`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link to /#`/search?q=&lt`;iframe src="javascript:..."&gt;. `filterTable` reads the q query parameter, marks it trusted with `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` and binds it to [`innerHTML`], so the payload executes in the victim's origin and can read the bearer token from `localStorage`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```html
   9 │       <div>
  10 │         <span>{{ "TITLE_SEARCH_RESULTS" | translate }} - </span>
→ 11 │         <span id="searchValue" [innerHTML]="searchValue"></span>
  12 │       </div>
  13 │     } @else {
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-193](#m-193) — Remove sanitizer bypass and bind query as text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-040"></a><a id="f-040"></a>
#### F-040 · Product descriptions trusted as HTML

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** Anyone who can change a product description, for example through the product API or another server-side flaw, can store HTML or script in it. `trustProductDescription` marks every description as trusted HTML for the product grid.

The server write path and its authorization were not inspected, and one part did not read the site where the description is rendered.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The loop at line 110 wraps each `tableData[i].description` from the product search API as SafeHtml, which turns off Angular's sanitizer wherever it is rendered.

```typescript
  108 │   trustProductDescription (tableData: any[]) { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
  109 │     for (let i = 0; i < tableData.length; i++) { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
→ 110 │       tableData[i].description = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(tableData[i].description) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line restfulXssChallenge
  111 │     } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
  112 │   } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-194](#m-194) — Remove the trust bypass for product descriptions

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-041"></a><a id="f-041"></a>
#### F-041 · DOM XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends the victim a link like #`/search?q=&lt`;iframe src="javascript:`alert(document.cookie)`"&gt;. `filterTable` reads q and marks it as trusted HTML.

The template binds it through `innerHTML`, so the script runs in the app origin, where it can read the bearer token in `localStorage`. Script runs in the victim's session, so the attacker can steal the bearer token and take over the account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
  141 │       }) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
  142 │       this.dataSource.filter = queryParam.toLowerCase()
→ 143 │       this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line localXssChallenge xssBonusChallenge
  144 │       if (this.gridDataSourceSubscription) {
  145 │         this.gridDataSourceSubscription.unsubscribe()
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-195](#m-195) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟠 [F-041](#f-041))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-042"></a><a id="f-042"></a>
#### F-042 · Reflected XSS

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.html:9`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim /#`/track-result?id=&lt`;iframe src="javascript:..."&gt;. The server echoes the unknown id back as `orderId`, and the client wraps it with `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` and renders it via [`innerHtml`], executing script in the victim's session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```html
   7 │ <mat-card appearance="outlined" class="mat-elevation-z6 mat-own-card">
   8 │   <div class="mdc-card">
→  9 │     <h1><span translate>TITLE_SEARCH_RESULTS</span> - <span [innerHtml]="results.orderNo"></span></h1>
  10 │     <div class="delivery-status">
  11 │       <h3 translate>LABEL_EXPECTED_DELIVERY</h3>
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-196](#m-196) — Render orderId as text instead of bypassed HTML

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-027"></a><a id="f-027"></a>
#### F-027 · Dependency lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.npmrc:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-051](#w-051) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** Because `.npmrc` sets lockfile generation off, no `package-lock.json` is ever produced or committed. Every npm install in developer, CI, release and Docker builds therefore resolves semver ranges fresh against the public registry.

An attacker who publishes a malicious version within an allowed range, through maintainer account takeover or typosquatted transitive packages, gets it installed into builds and shipped artifacts. A malicious or compromised dependency version can reach release artifacts and container images without any reviewable change in the repository.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `.npmrc` line 1 disables package-lock generation repository-wide, so dependency resolution floats on every install.

```
→ 1 │ package-lock=false
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-183](#m-183) — Commit lockfiles and install from them

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

### 🟡 Medium (90)

<a id="t-121"></a><a id="f-121"></a>
#### F-121 · Card numbers and TOTP secrets stored unencrypted

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:38`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-034](#w-034) - Card numbers and TOTP secrets stored unencrypted (`card.ts:38`)

**Issue:** `Card.cardNum` and `User.totpSecret` are plain columns with no field-level encryption, and SQLite stores them in a plaintext file. Anyone who reads the database file or query results gets full card numbers and the seeds needed to bypass 2FA.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `card.ts:38` stores `cardNum` as a plain INTEGER, `user.ts:112` stores `totpSecret` as a plain STRING, and `models/index.ts:41` shows a plain SQLite file.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-262](#m-262) — Encrypt card numbers and TOTP secrets

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-122"></a><a id="f-122"></a>
#### F-122 · TOTP secrets stored unencrypted in the Users table

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:112`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-035](#w-035) - TOTP secrets stored unencrypted in the Users table (`user.ts:112`)

**Issue:** The `totpSecret` column holds each user's 2FA seed as a plain STRING in the unencrypted SQLite file. An attacker who reads the table can generate valid one-time codes for every enrolled user, which defeats the second factor.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-263](#m-263) — Encrypt TOTP secrets with an external key

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-142"></a><a id="f-142"></a>
#### F-142 · Hardcoded Ethereum wallet mnemonic exposes private key

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` holds a BIP-39 mnemonic as a string literal, and the server derives the wallet's private key from it in process on each request. Anyone who can read the repository, the deployed source or the built bundle gets the seed phrase, derives the private key, and controls every asset at that wallet address.

They can also submit the key to `/rest/web3/submitKey` to pass the server's key check.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-283](#m-283) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (🟡 [F-142](#f-142))

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-144"></a><a id="f-144"></a>
#### F-144 · Confidential records stored in an unencrypted SQLite file

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - SQLite Embedded Store  ·  **Location:** `models/index.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-036](#w-036) - Confidential records stored in an unencrypted SQLite file (`index.ts:41`)

**Issue:** An attacker who can read files in the application's working directory (for example via a backup, an exposed volume, container image extraction or a separate file-read flaw) copies data/juiceshop.sqlite. They can then read every user, address, card and wallet record offline, because the file has no encryption at rest and no database-level authentication.

Anyone holding a copy of the database file can read all Confidential application data offline.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `createSequelize` sets storage to the relative plaintext path 'data/juiceshop.sqlite' with dialect sqlite and placeholder credentials, and configures no encryption.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-285](#m-285) — Encrypt the SQLite database

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-145"></a><a id="f-145"></a>
#### F-145 · Testing account credentials hardcoded in frontend bundle

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:62`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The login component embeds the email (testing@juice-`sh.op`) and password of a testing account, so they ship in the public JavaScript bundle. Anyone who loads the SPA and reads the compiled login component gets these credentials and can log in as that account if it exists on the server.

The served sources do not show whether the account exists on the server.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:61-62` sets `testingUsername` 'testing@juice-`sh.op`' and `testingPassword` 'IamUsedForTesting' as public component fields.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-286](#m-286) — Remove embedded test credentials from client code

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-146"></a><a id="f-146"></a>
#### F-146 · Plaintext user account passwords embedded in client

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/hacking-instructor/challenges/exposedCredentials.ts:60`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An anonymous visitor opens the browser dev tools and searches `main.js`. The hacking-instructor modules contain account emails and passwords in plaintext, such as testing@juice-`sh.op` with IamUsedForTesting, admin123 for the admin account, and the passwords for bender and jim.

Anyone who reads the public JavaScript learns valid-looking credentials for privileged and regular accounts, which can lead to account takeover if the server accepts them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `exposedCredentials.ts:54` and :60 compare the login fields against testing@juice-`sh.op` and IamUsedForTesting. `passwordStrength.ts:73` contains admin123, `loginBender.ts:84` contains 'OhG0dPlease1nsertLiquor!' and `loginJim.ts:73` contains 'ncc-1701'. `index.ts` imports all of these into the shipped frontend.

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-287](#m-287) — Remove real credentials from frontend tutorial code

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-148"></a><a id="f-148"></a>
#### F-148 · TOTP secrets are stored in plaintext in the user table

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/2fa.ts:129`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-037](#w-037) - TOTP secrets are stored in plaintext in the user table (`2fa.ts:129`)

**Issue:** The 2FA setup handler writes the TOTP secret straight to the `Users.totpSecret` column. Anyone who reads the database, for example through the SQL injection in login, obtains every second-factor seed.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-263](#m-263) — Encrypt TOTP secrets with an external key

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-149"></a><a id="f-149"></a>
#### F-149 · Security answer HMAC key is hardcoded in source

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:42`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** The HMAC key protecting stored security answers is a literal in `lib/insecurity.ts`. An attacker with database access and the source can run an offline dictionary attack on the answers, which are low-entropy, and reset passwords.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◑ [M-289](#m-289) — Move the HMAC key to external secret storage

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-03](#tc-data-03)

<a id="t-081"></a><a id="f-081"></a>
#### F-081 · Reset-password rate limit keyed on spoofable client address

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:346`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `server.ts:342` enables 'trust proxy' globally and the reset-password limiter (line 346) keys on headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip.

Because `req.ip` is derived from the client-supplied X-Forwarded-For under trust proxy, an attacker rotates that header per request to brute-force security answers and take over accounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-227](#m-227) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟡 [F-081](#f-081))

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-082"></a><a id="f-082"></a>
#### F-082 · Unauthenticated review creation

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews` is registered without authentication and stores author from `req.body.author`. An anonymous attacker posts reviews attributed to any customer or admin email.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-228](#m-228) — Verify token signatures before trusting claims

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-083"></a><a id="f-083"></a>
#### F-083 · Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `getUserId` calls `security.decode`, but its implementation (verifying or not) is not in the supplied source.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** `getUserId` (`chat.ts:45`) uses `security.decode`, which only decodes the JWS payload without verifying it. `/rest/chat` has no `isAuthorized` middleware (`server.ts:638`), and `updateAuthenticatedUsers` only caches verified tokens without rejecting others.

An attacker forges an unsigned token with `data.id` of a victim; `getOrderById` then looks up the victim's email and returns the victim's orders, and the victim's username is placed in the system prompt.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-160](#m-160) — Manual review: verify Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature verification · ◑ [M-229](#m-229) — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat user id

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-084"></a><a id="f-084"></a>
#### F-084 · JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material), AC-T-003 (Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/verify.ts:120`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** An attacker sends a JWT whose header sets `HS256`, HMAC-signed with the RSA public key, or alg none. `jwt.verify` runs with only the public key and no algorithms allowlist, so the header decides how the token is verified.

Here a verification that relies on the token's own header gates challenge evaluation.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/verify.ts:120` calls `jwt.verify(token, security.publicKey, cb)` without an algorithms option. Lines 86 and 89 expect tokens with alg none and `HS256` to pass. Whether such a forged token passes depends on the jsonwebtoken version, which was not inspected.

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ● [M-179](#m-179) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-02](#tc-authn-02)

<a id="t-085"></a><a id="f-085"></a>
#### F-085 · No password strength policy on registration

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:19`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Registration and password change accept any non-empty password, such as '1'. Attackers can then take over accounts by guessing or credential stuffing, which the missing login rate limit makes easier.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `changePassword.ts:19-25` checks only that the new password is non-empty and matches repeat. `server.ts:410-412` only trims the registration password and checks that it is not empty.

**Fix:** Enforce a length and complexity policy and reject reused / breached passwords → ◑ [M-230](#m-230) — Enforce a server-side password strength policy

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-521](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/521.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-086"></a><a id="f-086"></a>
#### F-086 · Password change trusts cached tokens
