**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker who stole a victim's bearer token calls change-password after the token's 6-hour lifetime has passed. The handler resolves the user from the in-memory `authenticatedUsers` cache and never checks exp, iss or aud, so the expired token still lets the attacker change the victim's password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `changePassword.ts:27` takes the bearer token and line 33 calls `security.authenticatedUsers.get(token)`, an in-memory map lookup with no lifetime check. The read slices showed no code that evicts expired entries.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-231](#m-231) — Enforce token expiry on every authenticated lookup

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03)

<a id="t-087"></a><a id="f-087"></a>
#### F-087 · Client-supplied UserId trusted for feedback and complaint

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:402`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An attacker fetches and solves a captcha from GET `/rest/captcha`. The attacker then sends POST `/api/Feedbacks` with comment, rating, captcha and UserId set to another user's id.

One part adds that POST `/api/Complaints` (`server.ts:382`) checks only `isAuthorized` and does not set UserId on the server either.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-232](#m-232) — Derive UserId from the verified token on create

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-088"></a><a id="f-088"></a>
#### F-088 · CAPTCHA answer returned to the client with the challenge

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/captcha.ts:31`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** GET `/rest/captcha` responds with the whole captcha object including answer. A bot reads the answer and submits it with `captchaId` to POST `/api/Feedbacks`, defeating the human-verification control and enabling automated feedback spam.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-233](#m-233) — Omit CAPTCHA answer and make CAPTCHAs single use

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-804](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/804.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-089"></a><a id="f-089"></a>
#### F-089 · Wallet address claims accepted without authentication

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/nftMint.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) - external → Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface · Authentication: The handler trusts `req.body.walletAddress` from internet callers without authentication or proof of ownership.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker watches the public Sepolia chain for NFTMinted or ContractExploited events and posts the emitting address as `walletAddress` to `/rest/web3/walletNFTVerify` or `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress`. The server never asks for a signature proving the caller controls that address, so it marks the challenge solved for the attacker.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-234](#m-234) — Require signed nonce proving wallet ownership

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-091"></a><a id="f-091"></a>
#### F-091 · Server trusts client-asserted challenge

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Without performing the XSS or closing notifications in a browser, an attacker emits 'verifyLocalXssChallenge' with the literal expected iframe string (or 'verifyCloseNotificationsChallenge' with a two-element array), and the server marks the challenge solved, persists it and broadcasts the solve.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-236](#m-236) — Verify challenge completion from server-side state

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-092"></a><a id="f-092"></a>
#### F-092 · No password strength policy on registration

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Users can set one-character or common passwords at registration and password reset. An attacker then guesses or sprays common passwords against the login endpoint to take over accounts. Weak passwords make accounts easy to take over by guessing.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `resetPassword` checks only that `newPassword` is non-empty and equals repeat (26-33) before `user.update` (44). The registration pre-check only requires `password.length` !== 0.

**Fix:** Enforce a length and complexity policy and reject reused / breached passwords → ◑ [M-230](#m-230) — Enforce a server-side password strength policy

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-521](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/521.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-04](#tc-authn-04)

<a id="t-104"></a><a id="f-104"></a>
#### F-104 · Stored profile image URL injected into CSP header

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:88`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A user posts an unreachable `imageUrl` such as `https://x`; script-src 'unsafe-inline'. On fetch failure the raw URL is stored as `profileImage` (`profileImageUrlUpload.ts:36`) and later concatenated into the `Content-Security-Policy` header, letting the attacker weaken the profile page CSP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-245](#m-245) — Never place stored user URLs into CSP directives

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-74](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/74.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-105"></a><a id="f-105"></a>
#### F-105 · Unauthenticated Socket\.IO events alter challenge state and broadcast flags

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Instances (2):** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts`: 34, 36

**Issue:** `registerWebsocketEvents.ts` accepts any socket connection without authentication, emits every stored challenge-solved notification (including CTF flags) to it, and lets any client delete notifications via 'notification received' (line 34) or trigger challenge solves via verify* events. A direct Socket\.IO client bypasses the browser CORS origin setting.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-246](#m-246) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (🟡 [F-105](#f-105))

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-107"></a><a id="f-107"></a>
#### F-107 · Fetched profile image is stored

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:29`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-046](#w-046) - Fetched profile image is stored without type or size checks

**Issue:** A logged-in user points `imageUrl` to a large file or to an SVG with script ending in .svg. The server streams the body to the public uploads folder with no size limit or content check. A .svg file served from the app origin can run script when opened directly.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Validate uploaded file type, size, and storage path; never execute uploaded content → ◑ [M-248](#m-248) — Validate fetched image content and cap its size

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-108"></a><a id="f-108"></a>
#### F-108 · Duplicate JSON keys bypass basket ownership check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-1  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:37`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → Juice Shop Express API · Authorization: Basket object id from request body is checked inconsistently against the authenticated user before persistence.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Issue:** `addBasketItem` parses the raw body with `parseJsonCustom`, checks ownership only against `basketIds[0]` but builds the item from `basketIds[last]`. Sending `{"BasketId":own,"BasketId":victim}` passes the check and inserts items into another user's basket; quantity checks also read `req.body`, which differs from the values used.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◕ [M-249](#m-249) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (🟡 [F-108](#f-108))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-109"></a><a id="f-109"></a>
#### F-109 · Basket coupon and basket item updates without owner check

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (2):** `routes/coupon.ts`: 18; `routes/basketItems.ts`: 68

**Issue:** At `routes/coupon.ts:18`, PUT `/rest/basket/:id/coupon/:coupon` loads the basket with `findByPk(id)` and overwrites its coupon with no ownership check. An authenticated user can pass another customer's basket ID and overwrite or clear that basket's coupon.

The parts disagree on the rejection status for coupon requests on baskets the caller does not own: one gives 404, the other 403.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-250](#m-250) — Verify basket ownership before applying coupon

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-110"></a><a id="f-110"></a>
#### F-110 · Client-supplied coupon data accepted for expired campaigns

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/order.ts:201`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** At checkout a user sends `couponData`=`base64('WMNSDY2019-&lt;validOn ms&gt;')`. The server only checks that the supplied date equals the campaign's fixed `validOn` value, never the current date, so anyone can apply expired 40-75% campaign discounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-251](#m-251) — Use trusted client IPs for rate limiting

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-807](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/807.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-111"></a><a id="f-111"></a>
#### F-111 · Basket item update accepts client-supplied BasketId

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:70`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Middleware at line 70 only records a challenge and passes body through, but the generated update handler that would persist BasketId was not read.

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Privileged attributes lack an enforced write allowlist

**Issue:** An authenticated user sends PUT `/api/BasketItems/{id}` with a body that sets BasketId to another user's basket. The middleware only records the mismatch for a challenge and calls `next()`, so the update can move the item into a basket the caller does not own.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** ◑ [M-161](#m-161) — Manual review: verify Basket item update accepts client-supplied BasketId · ◑ [M-252](#m-252) — Allow-list updatable basket item fields

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-112"></a><a id="f-112"></a>
#### F-112 · Product review author taken from request

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - verified attack-chain keystone in MODEL-AC-002 (Review posted or altered in another user's name); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/createProductReviews.ts:26`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Any caller, without a token, sends PUT `/rest/products/1/reviews` with author set to another user's e-mail. The handler stores the body author verbatim, so reviews can be forged in any user's name.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:633` registers the route without a guard. `createProductReviews.ts:26` stores author: `req.body.author`.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-253](#m-253) — Derive review author from the verified session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-113"></a><a id="f-113"></a>
#### F-113 · Feedback creation accepts a client-supplied owner UserId

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:406`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** A caller sends POST `/api/Feedbacks` with a UserId of another user. The finale Feedback resource binds the body, and the model accepts UserId unchanged. Feedback is therefore attributed to a victim; the `forgedFeedbackChallenge` check only detects the mismatch and does not block it.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-254](#m-254) — Set feedback owner from the authenticated session

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-114"></a><a id="f-114"></a>
#### F-114 · Stored user-written reviews returned as tool results

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:149`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-020](#w-020) - Stored user-written reviews returned as tool results into model context

**Issue:** LLM01/ASI01 indirect injection: anyone can store review text via the unauthenticated PUT `/rest/products/:id/reviews` (`createProductReviews.ts:25`). `getProductReviews` returns those documents unmodified as tool results, so instructions embedded in a review can redirect another user's chat session, e.g. to issue coupons or disclose prompt content.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-255](#m-255) — Return review content as delimited untrusted data

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-115"></a><a id="f-115"></a>
#### F-115 · Caller-supplied chat history and roles passed unvalidated to the model

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `routes/chat.ts`: 206, 191

**Issue:** LLM01 Prompt Injection / ASI01: `chat.ts:191` takes `req.body.messages` verbatim and passes it to `streamText`. A caller can supply system or assistant messages, fabricated tool results or prior turns that override the coupon policy and other rules.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-256](#m-256) — Validate chat messages to user-role text only

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-119"></a><a id="f-119"></a>
#### F-119 · Client-side coupon validation trusts a forgeable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/payment/payment.component.ts:160`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-023](#w-023) - Client-side coupon validation trusts a forgeable client date

**Issue:** An attacker picks an expired campaign code from the hardcoded campaigns table in the bundle, such as WMNSDY2019. They then change the browser clock or edit `sessionStorage` so the client builds `couponDetails` as '&lt;code&gt;-&lt;`validOn`&gt;' and shows a 75% discount.

Unauthorized discounts on orders, causing financial loss for the shop if the server accepts the client-supplied campaign date.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `payment.component.ts` lines 84-94 hardcode the campaign codes with their `validOn` timestamps. Line 160 writes the coupon and the client-computed date to `sessionStorage`, and line 163 validates the campaign against the client date. `order-summary.component.ts` line 79 sends this value to checkout. The server-side handling in the checkout route was not read.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-260](#m-260) — Validate coupons and campaign dates server-side only

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-123"></a><a id="f-123"></a>
#### F-123 · FTP file allow-list is checked before null-byte

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** A caller requests `/ftp/package.json.bak%2500.md`. The name passes the .md allow-list and is then truncated at the null byte, so the server returns backup and other non-allow-listed files from the ftp folder that the directory listing reveals.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-264](#m-264) — Canonicalize the file name before the allow-list check

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-180](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/180.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-124"></a><a id="f-124"></a>
#### F-124 · Math CAPTCHA endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/captcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker calls GET `/rest/captcha`, reads the answer field from the JSON, and submits it with feedback, defeating the CAPTCHA that `verifyCaptcha` checks.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - captcha object including answer (line 27) is sent with `res.json` (line 31); `verifyCaptcha` compares `req.body.captcha` to the stored answer (line 38).

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-265](#m-265) — Return only the CAPTCHA challenge, never its answer

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-125"></a><a id="f-125"></a>
#### F-125 · Confidential discount policy in system prompt can

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:105`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AI-05](#tc-ai-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-030](#w-030) - Confidential discount policy in system prompt can be extracted (`chat.ts:105`)

**Issue:** A caller sends messages asking the model to repeat its instructions, or claims to escalate a complaint. The model reveals the confidential internal 15% courtesy-discount instruction, or acts on it. Model text streams back unfiltered, and no server check governs the 15% benefit.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `chat.ts:105` embeds 'CONFIDENTIAL - INTERNAL ONLY' business policy in the system prompt. Line 205 sends it with caller messages, and line 218 writes the model's text deltas to the client with no output filter.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-266](#m-266) — Remove confidential policy from the model prompt

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-AI-05](#tc-ai-05)

<a id="t-126"></a><a id="f-126"></a>
#### F-126 · Whoami JSONP callback lets other origins read

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/currentUser.ts:58`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-031](#w-031) - Whoami JSONP callback lets other origins read the logged-in user's data

**Issue:** An attacker page includes a script tag to `/rest/user/whoami?callback=steal`&`fields=email`,password; where the browser sends the token cookie, the JSONP response delivers the victim's email and requested fields to the attacker's callback.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - When `req.query.callback` is set the handler calls `res.jsonp(response)` (line 58), exposing cookie-authenticated user data to cross-origin script includes.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-267](#m-267) — Remove JSONP support from the whoami endpoint

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-127"></a><a id="f-127"></a>
#### F-127 · Image CAPTCHA endpoint returns the expected answer

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/imageCaptcha.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** A logged-in attacker or script calls GET `/rest/image-captcha`, reads answer from the JSON and passes `verifyImageCaptcha` for data export without solving the image.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `imageCaptcha` object includes answer: `captcha.text` (line 26) and is returned whole via `res.json` (line 31).

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-268](#m-268) — Strip the answer from the image CAPTCHA response

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-128"></a><a id="f-128"></a>
#### F-128 · Unauthenticated recycle lookup serializes other users'

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/recycles.ts:17`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker calls GET `/api/Recycles/[1,2,3,...]`. `JSON.parse` turns the path parameter into an array of ids, and the handler returns the full records, including UserId, AddressId, pickup flag and date, for any user.

Anyone can enumerate the recycle pickup schedules of all users and link them to user and address ids.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:388` registers `getRecycleItem` with no auth guard. `routes/recycles.ts:14` parses the id as JSON. `routes/recycles.ts:17` sends the whole query result with no owner filter or field selection.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◑ [M-269](#m-269) — Require auth and owner filter on recycle lookup

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-129"></a><a id="f-129"></a>
#### F-129 · Security question lookup reveals registered

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/securityQuestion.ts:21`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker queries GET `/rest/user/security-question?email=X`. The response contains a question for a registered email and an empty object otherwise. This lets the attacker enumerate accounts and learn which question protects password reset for each one.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `securityQuestion.ts:21-25` returns {question} when a security answer exists for the email and {} otherwise.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-270](#m-270) — Return uniform responses for unknown accounts

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-204](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/204.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-05](#tc-authn-05)

<a id="t-130"></a><a id="f-130"></a>
#### F-130 · Whoami fields parameter returns arbitrary user fields

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/currentUser.ts:31`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An attacker holding a session (or running script in the victim's browser) requests `/rest/user/whoami?fields=password`,`totpSecret`,`deluxeToken` and receives the stored password hash and secrets from the session user object.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Requested field names from `req.query.fields` (line 23) are copied from `user.data` (line 31); `user.data` holds the password hash, as `changePassword.ts:39` compares against it.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-271](#m-271) — Restrict whoami field selection to an allowlist

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-131"></a><a id="f-131"></a>
#### F-131 · User list exposes emails, login IPs and deluxe tokens of all

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:363`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** Any registered customer calls GET `/api/Users` with their own bearer token. The generated finale resource excludes only password and `totpSecret`, so the response lists every account's email, role, `lastLoginIp` and `deluxeToken`.

An attacker can enumerate all accounts, including admin emails and roles, last login IP addresses, and the deluxe membership tokens that `isDeluxe` compares.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:363` guards the route only with `isAuthorized`. `server.ts:484` excludes only password and `totpSecret`. `models/user.ts:100` and `models/user.ts:104` define `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp`, which are serialized for every user.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-272](#m-272) — Restrict user list to admins and select safe fields

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-132"></a><a id="f-132"></a>
#### F-132 · Complaint list returns every user's complaints to any

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:381`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An authenticated customer calls GET `/api/Complaints`. The generated finale resource has no field exclusion and no owner filter, so it returns all complaint records with UserId, message text and uploaded file path.

A customer can read other customers' complaint messages and attached file references, which can contain personal or order details.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:381` applies only `isAuthorized`. `server.ts:489` registers Complaint with exclude [] and no list hook. `models/complaint.ts:36` stores the complaint message, which is returned for every user.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-273](#m-273) — Scope complaint list to the requesting owner

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-133"></a><a id="f-133"></a>
#### F-133 · Access logs publicly listed and downloadable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/logfileServer.ts:14`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Unauthenticated listing and `sendFile` of logs/ are shown, but the log file contents (IPs, URLs, user agents) are not in the supplied source.

**Weakness:** [W-040](#w-040) - Access logs publicly listed and downloadable (`logfileServer.ts:14`)

**Issue:** `/support/logs` is registered with `serve-index` and `serveLogFiles` without authentication; anyone downloads combined access logs containing client IPs, URLs and user agents of all users.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Strip secrets and PII from every log sink and rotate any token that already leaked → ◑ [M-162](#m-162) — Manual review: verify Access logs publicly listed and downloadable · ◑ [M-274](#m-274) — Remove public log routes

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-532](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-134"></a><a id="f-134"></a>
#### F-134 · Access log directory publicly browsable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:281`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-041](#w-041) - Access log directory publicly browsable (`server.ts:281`)

**Issue:** `server.ts:281` mounts `serveIndex` on the logs directory at `/support/logs` and line 283 serves individual log files, with no authentication middleware in the inspected registration. Morgan combined logs (line 338) include client IPs, URLs and query strings of all users, which an anonymous visitor can download.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Strip secrets and PII from every log sink and rotate any token that already leaked → ◑ [M-275](#m-275) — Remove public log directory routes

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-532](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/532.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-135"></a><a id="f-135"></a>
#### F-135 · Access log files served to unauthenticated callers

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:283`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-043](#w-043) - Access log files served to unauthenticated callers (`server.ts:283`)

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker browses `/support/logs` and downloads access logs that record other users' request URLs, IPs and query-string data such as passwords sent to change-password.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `serveIndex` lists logs/ at `/support/logs` (line 281) and `serveLogFiles` sends any file from logs/ without a guard; log format config was not served.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-276](#m-276) — Remove public log access or require admin authentication

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-538](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/538.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-05](#tc-data-05)

<a id="t-136"></a><a id="f-136"></a>
#### F-136 · Encryption key directory publicly listed and served

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/keyServer.ts:14`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-048](#w-048) - Encryption key directory publicly listed and served (`keyServer.ts:14`)

**Issue:** `/encryptionkeys` is served with `serve-index` and `serveKeyFiles` with no authentication, so anyone can enumerate and download every file in the key directory used by the JWT logic.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-277](#m-277) — Remove public routes for the key directory

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-137"></a><a id="f-137"></a>
#### F-137 · Access log directory is publicly listed and downloadable

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:281`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-049](#w-049) - Access log directory is publicly listed and downloadable (`server.ts:281`)

**Issue:** An unauthenticated caller browses `/support/logs` and downloads the access logs. The logs reveal other users' request URLs, IP addresses and any tokens or parameters in query strings.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-278](#m-278) — Stop serving the logs directory over HTTP

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-552](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/552.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-01](#tc-file-01)

<a id="t-138"></a><a id="f-138"></a>
#### F-138 · Change-password sends current and new passwords

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/changePassword.ts:14`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-044](#w-044) - Change-password sends current and new passwords in the URL query

**Issue:** A user changes the password via GET `/rest/user/change-password?current=`..&`new=`..; the cleartext passwords land in access logs, proxy logs and browser history, where an attacker who reads the logs recovers them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Route registered as GET; passwords read from `query.current` and `query.new` (lines 14-15).

**Fix:** ◑ [M-279](#m-279) — Move password change to a POST body over TLS

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-598](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/598.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-04](#tc-data-04)

<a id="t-139"></a><a id="f-139"></a>
#### F-139 · File type allowlist bypass

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileServer.ts:28`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `/ftp/:file` checks the .md/.pdf suffix before `cutOffPoisonNullByte` strips everything after '%00'. Requesting `package.json.bak`%`2500.md` passes the check and then serves `package.json.bak` or any other ftp file unauthenticated.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-280](#m-280) — Normalize file name before allowlist check

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-626](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/626.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-140"></a><a id="f-140"></a>
#### F-140 · Order export and history keyed on lossy vowel-masked email instead of user ID

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (2):** `routes/dataExport.ts`: 33; `routes/orderHistory.ts`: 17

**Issue:** Orders are stored with the buyer's email after every vowel is replaced by '*'. The data export and the order history at `routes/orderHistory.ts:17` both look up orders in `ordersCollection` by the caller's masked email.

The order write path that stores masked emails was not read, and no part checked whether registration accepts such colliding addresses.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-281](#m-281) — Query orders by authenticated user ID

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-141"></a><a id="f-141"></a>
#### F-141 · Any authenticated user can list all users' complaints

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:381`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Complaints GET requires `isAuthorized` and Complaint is a finale resource, but any list-level filter hook past the supplied window is unseen.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated customer sends GET `/api/Complaints` (`server.ts:381`). The route checks only authentication through `isAuthorized`, and the generated finale Complaint list returns every row without filtering by owner.

Per-id access is blocked by `denyAll` on `/api/Complaints/:id`, but the list route has no such restriction.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-163](#m-163) — Manual review: verify Any authenticated user can list all users' complaints… · ◑ [M-282](#m-282) — Scope complaint listing to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-143"></a><a id="f-143"></a>
#### F-143 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Instances (2):** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts`: 30, 36

**Issue:** An attacker opens a socket\.io connection from a non-browser client (CORS origin restriction does not apply). On connection the server replays every stored notification, each containing the challenge's CTF flag, and later broadcasts every new solve via `io.emit` to all sockets, so the attacker harvests other players' flags.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `io.on('connection')` performs no auth and emits every notifications entry (with flag from `utils.ctfFlag`) to the new socket; `challengeUtils.ts:75` broadcasts new ones to all sockets.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-284](#m-284) — Authenticate socket connections before emitting flags

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-147"></a><a id="f-147"></a>
#### F-147 · Save login IP response serializes the full user

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/saveLoginIp.ts:33`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** GET `/rest/saveLoginIp` returns the updated UserModel instance as JSON. The response includes the `MD5` password hash, the TOTP secret and the `deluxeToken`.

Anyone who obtains the response, for example by script access to the non-HttpOnly token, gets material to crack the password and clone the second factor.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-288](#m-288) — Return only needed fields from saveLoginIp

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-01](#tc-data-01)

<a id="t-151"></a><a id="f-151"></a>
#### F-151 · B2B order evaluation blocks the event loop per request

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** POST `/b2b/v2/orders` evaluates attacker-supplied `orderLinesData` with notevil inside `vm.runInContext`, which runs synchronously with a 2000 ms timeout. An authenticated client sends looping expressions in parallel, stalling the single Node\.js thread for 2 s each and denying service to all users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-291](#m-291) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-152"></a><a id="f-152"></a>
#### F-152 · Unbounded synchronous parsing of uploaded XML

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:109`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-014](#w-014) - Unbounded synchronous parsing of uploaded XML and YAML (`fileUpload.ts:109`)

**Issue:** Unauthenticated callers repeatedly upload entity-expansion XML or YAML bombs; each parse runs synchronously in a vm context for up to 2 seconds, blocking the event loop, with no rate limit on `/file-upload`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-292](#m-292) — Offload CPU-bound work and bound execution time

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-153"></a><a id="f-153"></a>
#### F-153 · Zip upload extracted without decompressed size limits

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:28`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads a 200KB zip bomb. Every entry is streamed to disk with no limit on entry count or decompressed size, which fills the disk and degrades the service.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-293](#m-293) — Enforce entry count and decompressed size limits

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-409](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/409.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-154"></a><a id="f-154"></a>
#### F-154 · Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:203`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: `streamText` lacks `maxOutputTokens`, but body-size limits and any global rate limiting are not shown in supplied source.

**Weakness:** [W-015](#w-015) - Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint (`chat.ts:203`)

**Issue:** LLM10: `/rest/chat` is registered without authentication or rate limiting (`server.ts:638`). `streamText` (`chat.ts:203`) sets no `maxOutputTokens` and no message count/length bound; only `stepCountIs(10)` and configured retries apply.

Any caller can loop requests with long histories to exhaust provider quota and cost.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-164](#m-164) — Manual review: verify Unbounded LLM consumption on unauthenticated chat endpoint · ◑ [M-294](#m-294) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (🟡 [F-154](#f-154))

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-155"></a><a id="f-155"></a>
#### F-155 · Unbounded in-memory wallet address set fed

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/web3Wallet.ts:16`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) - external → Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface · Validation: The body field `walletAddress` crosses from the internet into a process-wide Set without validation.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker repeatedly POSTs `/rest/web3/walletExploitAddress` with distinct large `walletAddress` strings. Each value is added to the module-level `walletsConnected` Set without validation, size cap or expiry, and entries are removed only when a matching on-chain event arrives.

Process memory grows until the server degrades or crashes.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-295](#m-295) — Validate wallet addresses and cap the tracked set

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-156"></a><a id="f-156"></a>
#### F-156 · Memory photo upload has no size limit and trusts client MIME

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:698`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-016](#w-016) - Memory photo upload has no size limit and trusts client MIME type

**Issue:** A caller with a token posts very large files to `/rest/memories`. The disk-storage multer instance has no size limit, so the files fill the public uploads folder. Non-image content declared as image/png is also stored and served.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-296](#m-296) — Add size limits and content sniffing to disk uploads

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-770](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/770.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · Verification items: [TC-FILE-02](#tc-file-02)

<a id="t-157"></a><a id="f-157"></a>
#### F-157 · Attacker-controlled \$where JavaScript blocks

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**Reliability:** Low. Not confirmed, this may be a false positive: Concatenation into \$where and `global.sleep` are shown, but whether `noSqlCommandChallenge` is enabled at runtime (selecting the vulnerable branch) is not evidenced.

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Document-database queries take their structure from application values

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) - Juice Shop Express API → marsdb Embedded Store · Query construction: The review id path parameter is interpreted as JavaScript in a MarsDB \$where selector on the reviews collection.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/rest/products/sleep`(2000)`/reviews` repeatedly. With `noSqlCommandChallenge` enabled the 40-character id is concatenated into a \$where predicate, and the globally installed blocking sleep busy-waits the single Node event loop per evaluated review, stalling all requests.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◑ [M-165](#m-165) — Manual review: verify Attacker-controlled \$where JavaScript blocks event loop · ◑ [M-297](#m-297) — Replace \$where with numeric equality selector

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-158"></a><a id="f-158"></a>
#### F-158 · Regex with leading wildcard on unbounded

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:46`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker emits 'verifySvgInjectionChallenge' with a very long string lacking the '../../..' sequence; the unanchored pattern starting with .* backtracks quadratically on the single Node\.js event loop, stalling all requests.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `data?.match(/.*\.\.\/.../)` runs on any client string with no length check; actual stall depends on engine behavior and transport limits not shown.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-298](#m-298) — Bound payload length and remove leading wildcard from regex

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-1333](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1333.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-161"></a><a id="f-161"></a>
#### F-161 · B2B order lines evaluated by notevil inside node vm

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/b2bOrder.ts:23`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** An attacker posts `orderLinesData` to the B2B order endpoint. The value is evaluated as JavaScript by notevil inside node:vm. node:vm is not a security boundary, and the 2-second timeout lets each request block the event loop.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ◑ [M-299](#m-299) — Parse order lines as JSON instead of evaluating

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-03](#tc-input-03)

<a id="t-162"></a><a id="f-162"></a>
#### F-162 · Basket item creation bypasses basket ownership

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/basketItems.ts:42`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker posts JSON to `/api/BasketItems` with BasketId twice: first their own basket id, then a victim's. The ownership check compares only the first value with `user.bid`, but the saved item uses the last value, so the attacker adds items to the victim's basket.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Raw body keys are collected into arrays (lines 26-34). The check uses `basketIds[0]` (line 37), the build uses `basketIds[basketIds.length - 1]` (line 42), and the item is saved (line 47-49).

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-300](#m-300) — Derive BasketId from the authenticated session server-side

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-163"></a><a id="f-163"></a>
#### F-163 · Chatbot order lookup binds ownership by lossy

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:169`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AI-04](#tc-ai-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** The `getOrderById` tool of the chatbot fetches any order by `orderId`. At `routes/chat.ts:169` it accepts the order when `order.email` equals the caller's email with vowels replaced by '*'.

An authenticated user is required (`chat.ts:160`), but ownership is compared on a masked, non-unique email (`chat.ts:165`,169).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `chat.ts:166` looks up the order by `orderId` alone. `chat.ts:165`/169 compare it to a vowel-masked e-mail, which many accounts share. The order storage format and the model's willingness to call the tool were not verified.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-301](#m-301) — Bind order lookups to the caller's user id

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AI-04](#tc-ai-04)

<a id="t-164"></a><a id="f-164"></a>
#### F-164 · Any authenticated user can edit other users'

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker sends PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with the _id of another user's review and a new message. The handler updates the review by _id alone and never compares the review author with the caller, so the attacker rewrites the victim's review.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `updateProductReviews` resolves the user (line 16) but uses only `req.body.id` as the selector (line 18) and \$set message (line 19). It applies no author filter.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-302](#m-302) — Bind review updates to the authenticated author

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-165"></a><a id="f-165"></a>
#### F-165 · Authenticated users can read and delete other users' basket

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:359`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** The generated GET and DELETE `/api/BasketItems/:id` routes are guarded only by `isAuthorized` (`server.ts:359`). The finale handlers load or delete the BasketItem by primary key without comparing its BasketId with the caller's basket.

A logged-in user enumerates IDs to view or remove items in other customers' baskets.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:359` applies only `isAuthorized` to `/api/BasketItems/:id`. `finale.resource` at `server.ts:501` serves read and delete by id, and `models/basketitem.ts:31` shows the BasketId that no handler compares.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-303](#m-303) — Bind basket item access to the caller's basket

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01)

<a id="t-166"></a><a id="f-166"></a>
#### F-166 · Any authenticated user can delete any customer feedback

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:361`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** DELETE `/api/Feedbacks/:id` is guarded only by `isAuthorized` (`server.ts:361`), and the generated finale delete handler compares neither the feedback's UserId nor the caller's role. A logged-in customer deletes other users' feedback by ID, which is an action the client offers only in the administration view.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:361` applies only `isAuthorized` to `/api/Feedbacks/:id`. `finale.resource` at `server.ts:501` then deletes by primary key, while `models/feedback.ts:31` holds the owner UserId that is never compared.

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-304](#m-304) — Restrict feedback deletion to admins or the owner

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-167"></a><a id="f-167"></a>
#### F-167 · Server access logs downloadable without any authorization

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:283`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker requests `/support/logs/access.log`.`<date>`. The route serves files from the logs directory with no authentication or role check, which exposes other users' request URLs, IP addresses and user agents, data meant for operators only.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:281-283` registers a directory listing and `serveLogFiles` without a guard. Morgan writes combined access logs to logs/ (`server.ts:331`, 338).

**Fix:** ◑ [M-305](#m-305) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (🟡 [F-167](#f-167))

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02)

<a id="t-168"></a><a id="f-168"></a>
#### F-168 · Unauthenticated callers can rewrite challenge hint records

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:377`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** The `/api/Hints/:id` route registration guards only GET and DELETE (`server.ts:377-379`), so the generated finale PUT accepts any caller. The handler binds the whole body, so an attacker can change text, order, unlocked or ChallengeId of the global hints that all users see.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:377-379` omits PUT, and `finale.resource` at `server.ts:501` serves updates. `models/hint.ts:19-23` shows the text, ChallengeId and unlocked fields. The intended client use of PUT, unlocking a hint, was not inspected.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-306](#m-306) — Allow only the unlock field on hint updates

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-02](#tc-authz-02), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-169"></a><a id="f-169"></a>
#### F-169 · Privacy request owner taken from client-supplied UserId

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:444`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Issue:** An authenticated user sends POST `/api/PrivacyRequests` with a body such as `{"UserId": &lt;victim id&gt;, "deletionRequested": true}`. The route applies only `isAuthorized()`, which verifies the JWT, and not `security.appendUserId()`.

Forged privacy and deletion requests are attributed to arbitrary users. The downstream processing of these records was not inspected.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - POST `/api/PrivacyRequests` applies only `isAuthorized()`, and the generic finale resource persists the request body, including UserId.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-307](#m-307) — Bind PrivacyRequest UserId to the authenticated user

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHZ-01](#tc-authz-01), [TC-AUTHZ-03](#tc-authz-03)

<a id="t-093"></a><a id="f-093"></a>
#### F-093 · Token checks

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker replays an old or leaked token, such as an expired accounting token or a `password_valid_needs_second_factor` `tmpToken`. `jws.verify` checks only the signature and the callers never compare exp, so the expired token still grants the accounting role or completes 2FA login.

Captured tokens stay usable indefinitely for role checks and for second-factor completion.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `verify()` wraps `jws.verify` (line 55). `isAccounting` (156), `isDeluxe` (166), `isCustomer` (171) and 2fa verify then use `decode()` with no exp, iss or aud comparison.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-237](#m-237) — Validate exp, iss and aud wherever tokens are accepted

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03)

<a id="t-094"></a><a id="f-094"></a>
#### F-094 · Server-side token map sessions never expire or get

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:178`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** `appendUserId()` trusts any token present in `authenticatedUsers.tokenMap` without checking the JWT exp claim, and nothing removes entries. A stolen token keeps working on `/api/Cards`, `/api/Addresss` and wallet routes after its 6h expiry and after logout.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-238](#m-238) — Verify JWT expiry before tokenMap lookups

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-095"></a><a id="f-095"></a>
#### F-095 · Cached session tokens are trusted after their expiry

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:189`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03))

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains a once-valid token (the token cookie is set without `httpOnly` at line 192) keeps using it after its 6h expiry. `updateAuthenticatedUsers` skips verification for tokens already in `tokenMap`, and `appendUserId` takes the user id from `tokenMap` with no time check, so card and address routes accept the expired token.

Session tokens never expire for routes guarded only by `appendUserId`.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 188 only verifies when the token is not yet cached. Line 191 caches the token forever. Line 178 uses `tokenMap[token].data.id` with no exp check.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-239](#m-239) — Expire cached tokens and recheck exp on each use

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-AUTHN-03](#tc-authn-03)

<a id="t-120"></a><a id="f-120"></a>
#### F-120 · Payment card numbers stored in plaintext in SQLite

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:38`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-033](#w-033) - Payment card numbers stored in plaintext in SQLite (`card.ts:38`)

**Issue:** The Card model stores the full 16-digit `cardNum` as a plain INTEGER column in the unencrypted SQLite file data/juiceshop.sqlite. Anyone who reads the database file or extracts the table, for example through file disclosure, a backup or SQL injection elsewhere, recovers every card number with expiry and holder name.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Encrypt the data in transit and at rest with vetted primitives → ◑ [M-261](#m-261) — Encrypt or tokenize stored card numbers

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-311](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/311.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-DATA-02](#tc-data-02)

<a id="t-150"></a><a id="f-150"></a>
#### F-150 · Session token cookie lacks HttpOnly and Secure flags

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:192`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-028](#w-028) - Sensitive cookies remain accessible to browser scripts

**Issue:** `updateAuthenticatedUsers` sets the JWT into a 'token' cookie with default options, so any XSS in the application can read the bearer token via `document.cookie` and the cookie may be sent over plain HTTP.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Set `HttpOnly` on every session cookie → ◑ [M-290](#m-290) — Set secure session-cookie attributes

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1004](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1004.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-170"></a><a id="f-170"></a>
#### F-170 · Any authenticated user can list all user

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-09](#c-09) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/authenticatedUsers.ts:25`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** A self-registered customer calls GET `/rest/user/authentication-details` with their token. The route is only guarded by `isAuthorized()`, and the handler returns every user's `dataValues` (email, role, `deluxeToken`, `lastLoginIp`, profile) plus last login times.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-308](#m-308) — Restrict user listing to admin role

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-090"></a><a id="f-090"></a>
#### F-090 · Open redirect

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Instances (2):** `routes/redirect.ts`: 19; `lib/insecurity.ts`: 136

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a link to `/redirect?to`=`https://evil.example/?x=https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop`. `isRedirectAllowed` accepts any URL that merely contains an allowlisted entry, so `res.redirect` sends the victim to the attacker's phishing site under the shop's domain.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-235](#m-235) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-096"></a><a id="f-096"></a>
#### F-096 · Product description stored without HTML sanitization

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/product.ts:45`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** When `restfulXssChallenge` is enabled, the Product description setter only checks for a payload and stores the raw HTML. A user who can update a product can then persist script-bearing markup.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 45 branches on the challenge flag and line 55 stores the unsanitized description. Neither the route that updates products nor the browser rendering sink was read.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-240](#m-240) — Always sanitize product descriptions before storage

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-097"></a><a id="f-097"></a>
#### F-097 · User username and email setters apply weak or no HTML

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Sequelize application data access  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:49`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** When `persistedXssUserChallenge` is enabled, the username passes only through the regex-based `sanitizeLegacy` filter, which can be bypassed, and the email is stored raw. An attacker can persist HTML or script in a user record.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `user.ts:49` uses `sanitizeLegacy`, and `user.ts:60-70` stores the email unsanitized while the challenge is on. The rendering sink was not read.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-241](#m-241) — Use recursive library sanitization for user fields

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-106"></a><a id="f-106"></a>
#### F-106 · Cookie-authenticated profile update

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Juice Shop Express API  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateUserProfile.ts:38`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-025](#w-025) - Cookie-authenticated changes lack request authenticity checks

**Issue:** A malicious page auto-submits a form POST to `/profile` with a chosen username. The handler authenticates solely from the token cookie and updates the username without any CSRF token or origin check; combined with juice-shop-api-002 this can plant an eval payload in the victim's profile.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◑ [M-247](#m-247) — Add CSRF token and SameSite=Strict token cookie
