**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-116"></a><a id="f-116"></a>
#### F-116 · Unencoded data export written to same-origin popup

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/data-export/data-export.component.ts:71`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** After a data export request, `frontend/src/app/data-export/data-export.component.ts:71` opens an about:blank popup in the app's origin. It writes the server-returned `userData` with `document.write` and no output encoding. The content of the server's export was not inspected.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `data-export.component.ts:70-71` assigns `data.userData` and writes it with `window.open('', '_blank').document.write(this.userData)`. The server export content was not read, so reachability of attacker markup is unproven.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-257](#m-257) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (🟡 [F-116](#f-116))

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-117"></a><a id="f-117"></a>
#### F-117 · DOM XSS

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Found by:** survey and verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** High. Probably real: a model review confirmed it, and the survey and verification each found it.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** A logged-in user sends a True-Client-IP header containing HTML or script to `/rest/saveLoginIp`. The server applies `sanitizeSecure` only when `httpHeaderXssChallenge` is disabled, so with the challenge enabled it stores the header unsanitized.

One part did not inspect how the server records the IP and states only that it may come from request headers.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 39 passes `payload.data.lastLoginIp` to `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`, and the template renders it with [`innerHTML`] at line 10. The server stores the raw true-client-ip header when the challenge is enabled. Only the user's own `lastLoginIp` is writable, so attacking another user needs a further flaw.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-258](#m-258) — Render lastLoginIp as text without sanitizer bypass

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-118"></a><a id="f-118"></a>
#### F-118 · Order ID from tracking response rendered

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - Angular Frontend  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.ts:48`

**Found by:** verification ([TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04))

**Reliability:** Medium. An automatic check confirmed part of the claim.

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker sends a victim a tracking link with a crafted id parameter. The component sends the id to the track-order API and wraps the returned `orderId` in code tags marked as trusted HTML. If the server echoes the id, the payload runs in the victim's browser.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The route query id (line 45) is sent to `trackOrderService`, and `results.data[0].orderId` is wrapped as trusted HTML at line 48.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-259](#m-259) — Display the order ID as escaped text

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · Verification items: [TC-INPUT-04](#tc-input-04)

<a id="t-098"></a><a id="f-098"></a>
#### F-098 · Release tag name interpolated into shell run step

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/update-news-www.yml:19`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-018](#w-018) - Application data builds operating-system commands

**Issue:** `update-news-www.yml` and its legacy twin expand \${{ `github.event.release.tag_name` }} directly into a single-quoted sed command in a run step. That job's checkout persisted `BOT_TOKEN` for a cross-repository push. Creating such a release requires write access to the repository.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace shell invocations with an argv-list API and validate every input → ◑ [M-242](#m-242) — Pass release tag via env variable, not expression

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-78](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/78.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-099"></a><a id="f-099"></a>
#### F-099 · Missing Container Image Signing

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authentication relies on hand-rolled token verification

**Issue:** Missing Container Image Signing: Unsigned container images cannot be verified for provenance - any registry intermediary could substitute them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-148](#m-148) — Sign container images and verify signatures before use

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-04](#tc-supply-04) · Rules: IAC-040

<a id="t-100"></a><a id="f-100"></a>
#### F-100 · Heroku CLI installed by piping a remote script to sh in deploy job

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:358`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** The heroku deploy job runs `curl https://cli-assets.heroku.com/install.sh | sh` with no checksum or signature check. The next step in the same job receives `HEROKU_API_KEY`.

If the install script or its CDN is compromised, it can plant a tool that captures the API key or alters the production deployment.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-243](#m-243) — Install Heroku CLI from pinned verified package

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-494](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/494.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-101"></a><a id="f-101"></a>
#### F-101 · Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-027](#w-027) - Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 4; `package.json`: 56

**Issue:** Dependency Install Scripts Run at Build Time: Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-149](#m-149) — Install with npm ci --ignore-scripts in `Dockerfile` builds

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-03](#tc-supply-03) · Rules: IAC-005

<a id="t-102"></a><a id="f-102"></a>
#### F-102 · Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tag or branch

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to High because verified finding evidence ties the weakness to confirmed internet ingress tb-3  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-051](#w-051) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-3](#tb-3) 🌐 External - external → CI/CD Pipeline · Validation: Breaks the boundary assumption that every third-party action is pinned to an immutable SHA; @main runs with `GITHUB_TOKEN`.<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-3 raised this finding: Medium → High._

**Instances (3):** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 33; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 188; `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml`: 23

**Issue:** Workflows reference actions by mutable tags or branches. At `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33` the workflow runs calibreapp/image-actions@main with the `GITHUB_TOKEN`, together with peter-evans/create-pull-request@v8 and actions/checkout@v6.

Most other actions in `ci.yml` and `release.yml` are pinned to full commit SHAs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◕ [M-244](#m-244) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-011

<a id="t-103"></a><a id="f-103"></a>
#### F-103 · Container base images referenced by floating tags without digest

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Found by:** survey and scanners

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-026](#w-026) - Container base images referenced by floating tags without digest (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile`: 1; `test/smoke/Dockerfile`: 1

**Issue:** `Dockerfile:1` builds from node:24 and gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13, and `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1` uses alpine, all by mutable tag without a digest. If a malicious publisher or a registry change replaces the tag, a compromised or unexpected base image silently goes into the published bkimminich/juice-shop images.

The existing control is a multi-stage build with a distroless, non-root runtime image.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-166](#m-166) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-SUPPLY-01](#tc-supply-01) · Rules: IAC-001

<a id="t-159"></a><a id="f-159"></a>
#### F-159 · Container Runs as Root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Issue:** Container Runs as Root: Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-150](#m-150) — Run the smoke-test container as a non-root user

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-INFRA-01](#tc-infra-01) · Rules: IAC-002

<a id="t-160"></a><a id="f-160"></a>
#### F-160 · Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (13)

**Reliability:** Medium. A model review confirmed it, but the survey and verification did not both find it.

**Weakness:** [W-024](#w-024) - Missing Workflow Permissions Block

**Instances (13):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: 25; `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml`: 13; `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml`: 21; `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml`: 6; `.github/workflows/rebase.yml`: 8; `.github/workflows/release.yml`: 10; `.github/workflows/stale.yml`: 7; `.github/workflows/update-challenges-ebook.yml`: 11 … (+5 more)

**Issue:** Missing Workflow Permissions Block: Without an explicit permissions block at workflow or job level, the job inherits the repository default (commonly write-all) for `GITHUB_TOKEN` - any compromised step can push code, create releases, or approve PRs.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-151](#m-151) — Restrict GITHUB_TOKEN with explicit workflow permissions

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/) · Verification items: [TC-CI-01](#tc-ci-01) · Rules: IAC-010

_**Reliability** says how sure this report is that a finding is real, not how severe it is. It is a judgement, not a probability._

<a id="ac-b-002"></a>
### AC-B-002 — User gains more rights than intended

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration | Check whether a user can give themselves or<br/>others a role or permission beyond what<br/>their own role or delegation allows. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-172](#m-172) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Scenario impact**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied role field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-172](#m-172) — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role (**P1**): addresses 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-b-001"></a>
### AC-B-001 — User reads or changes data of another user or tenant

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review | Check whether a user can read or change data<br/>that belongs to another user or tenant,<br/>through the primary operation or through an<br/>export, download, share link, search, or<br/>report. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-167](#m-167) — Scope basket query to authenticated user (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-b-003"></a>
### AC-B-003 — User takes over another account

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — Unauthenticated password reset relies only on a security answer | Check whether anyone other than the verified<br/>owner can change an account's credentials or<br/>recovery data, such as email, phone number,<br/>or reset tokens. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-182](#m-182) — Require emailed one-time reset token (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — Unauthenticated password reset relies only on a security answer at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Stored username evaluated as JavaScript on profile | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Scenario impact**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-190](#m-190) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (🟠 [F-036](#f-036)) (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Stored username evaluated as JavaScript on profile at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-007"></a>
### AC-T-007 — Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Make the model invoke a privileged tool or return data the attacker is not entitled to, using the application's own authority.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can place text into a conversation, document, or record that the application later sends to a language model.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-078](#f-078) — Prompt injection makes chatbot issue coupons with arbitrary | The handler builds the prompt by<br/>interpolating request or stored text into<br/>the same string as the system instructions,<br/>so attacker text is read as instruction. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-073](#f-073) — Chatbot generateCoupon tool issues arbitrary discounts | Tools exposed to the model run with the<br/>application's authority; a manipulated model<br/>turn therefore performs actions the caller<br/>could not. |

**Scenario impact**

Text an attacker can place anywhere the application later feeds to the model becomes instruction, and the tools behind that model run with the application's authority rather than the caller's - so a comment or a stored record reaches actions the attacker was never entitled to.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-224](#m-224) — Enforce coupon policy server-side (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-078](#f-078) — Prompt injection makes chatbot issue coupons with arbitrary at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ◕ [M-220](#m-220) — Enforce coupon limits and eligibility in tool code (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-073](#f-073) — Chatbot generateCoupon tool issues arbitrary discounts at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-b-005"></a>
### AC-B-005 — User exceeds a limit or a one-time entitlement

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Wallet top-up amount accepted from client | Check whether a one-time or limited<br/>entitlement, such as a coupon, voucher,<br/>quota, or balance, can be used more often or<br/>for more value than allowed, including by<br/>repeating or racing the operation. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-184](#m-184) — Validate amount and settle payment before crediting wallet (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Wallet top-up amount accepted from client at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-b-007"></a>
### AC-B-007 — User misuses a privileged function for another purpose

> **Source:** business abuse case · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-069](#f-069) — Any authenticated user can list all user accounts | Check whether a user can make a privileged<br/>function, such as support impersonation, an<br/>administrative action, or a background job,<br/>act on resources or perform operations<br/>outside that user's own authority. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-217](#m-217) — Require admin role for the user list endpoint (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-069](#f-069) — Any authenticated user can list all user accounts at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-178](#m-178) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (**P1**): addresses 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.
- ● [M-179](#m-179) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call (**P1**): addresses 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm at **Step 2**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="ac-t-003"></a>
### AC-T-003 — Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge an admin-role JWT without knowledge of the signing secret.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can obtain any valid JWT issued by the system (e.g. by registering a free account).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm | Verifier accepts attacker-chosen `alg` (e.g.<br/>`none` or HMAC-with-public-key), allowing<br/>token re-signing without the secret. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`lib/insecurity.ts:157` | Forged `role: admin` claim is accepted as<br/>authoritative because the role is not<br/>re-fetched from the database per request. |

**Scenario impact**

Algorithm confusion alone yields a forgeable token; trusting the in-token role claim turns that forgery into instant admin access - neither gap is Critical in isolation, but the chain is a full authentication bypass.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ● [M-179](#m-179) — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call (**P1**): addresses 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="model-ac-002"></a>
### MODEL-AC-002 — Review posted or altered in another user's name

> **Source:** business abuse case, model-derived · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟡 Medium · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-112](#f-112) — Product review author taken from request | Check whether a user can create, edit or<br/>like a product review attributed to another<br/>user, or modify a review that belongs to<br/>someone else. |

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◑ [M-253](#m-253) — Derive review author from the verified session (**P3**): addresses 🟡 [F-112](#f-112) — Product review author taken from request at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

<a id="model-ac-001"></a>
### MODEL-AC-001 — Wallet NFT minting without proof of wallet ownership

> **Source:** business abuse case, model-derived · **Actor:** attacker · **Combined Risk:** not rated (no linked finding) · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|---------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | _no matching finding_<br/>`routes/nftMint.ts:41` | Check whether an NFT can be minted or<br/>claimed for a wallet address the requester<br/>has not proven they control, or minted more<br/>than once where only one claim is allowed. |

---

<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** technical abuse case · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ◐ Partially blocked

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Proposed fixes for confirmed steps**

- ◕ [M-167](#m-167) — Scope basket query to authenticated user (**P2**): addresses 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review at **Step 1**. Validate the fix against this step before treating the chain as blocked.

---

## 10. Mitigation Register

Changes are ordered by priority: P1 before deployment, P2 this sprint, P3 next quarter, P4 backlog. Each entry links its findings and gives the available implementation steps and checks.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-168"></a>
#### M-168 — Use parameterized replacements for search criteria

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-002](#f-002) — SQL injection in product search query

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the template string with `sequelize.query` using LIKE :q and replacements: { q: `%${criteria}%` }, or use `ProductModel.findAll` with `Op.like`.
2. Validate q as a bounded string and reject non-string values before building the query.

**Verification:** Run GET `/rest/products/search?q=qwert`')) `UNION SELECT id,email,password,4,5,6,7,8,9 FROM Users`-- and confirm the response is an empty or normal product list with no user rows and no SQL error.

**Reference:** [CWE-89: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements used in an SQL Command (SQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html)

---

<a id="m-169"></a>
#### M-169 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (F-008)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Insecure Direct Object Reference

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/payment.ts:21`

**How:** Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id` (or equivalent session-derived identity) in every WHERE/filter clause.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-001 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/payment.ts:21`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-170"></a>
#### M-170 — Use parameterized queries for login lookup

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-009](#f-009) — SQL injection in login query enables authentication bypass

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace the raw template-literal query with `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email, password: security.hash(password), deletedAt: null } })`, or use `sequelize.query` with replacements or bind.
2. Check that email and password are strings of bounded length before the query, and reject other types.
3. Add a regression test that sends quote and comment payloads in the email field.

**Verification:** Send POST `/rest/user/login` with email "admin@juice-`sh.op`'--" and with "' OR 1=1--", each with a wrong password, and confirm each returns 401 with no token issued.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Query Parameterization Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Query_Parameterization_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-171"></a>
#### M-171 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-010)

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-010](#f-010) — Server-side eval of stored username on profile render

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Delete the eval branch in `getUserProfile` and always treat username as data.
2. Pass username to pug as a template local (`fn({ username })`) with escaped interpolation instead of string-replacing it into the template.
3. Validate username on POST `/profile` against a character allowlist and length limit.

**Verification:** POST `/profile` with username=#{1+1}, then GET `/profile` and confirm the page shows the literal text and not 2.

**Reference:** [CWE-95](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/95.html)

---

<a id="m-172"></a>
#### M-172 — Allowlist registration fields and force customer role

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-011](#f-011) — Mass assignment of admin role during user registration

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Add a `create.write.before` hook or custom handler on the User resource that copies only email, password and username from the request body.
2. Set role to 'customer' server-side, and ignore role, `deluxeToken`, `isActive`, `profileImage` and `totpSecret` from the request.
3. Restrict role changes to an admin-only endpoint with a server-side role check.

**Verification:** Automated test: POST `/api/Users` with role admin, then fetch the created user and assert that its role is 'customer'.

**Reference:** [CWE-915: Improperly Controlled Modification of Dynamically-Determined Object Attributes (Mass Assignment)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

---

<a id="m-178"></a>
#### M-178 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — JWT RSA private signing key embedded in source code

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:21`

**How:**

1. Remove the private key literal from `lib/insecurity.ts`.
2. Load the private key at runtime from a secret manager, a mounted secret or a protected file outside the repository.
3. Rotate the key pair, since the embedded key must be treated as compromised.
4. Invalidate all tokens signed with the old key.
5. Remove the hardcoded HMAC key on line 42 that is used for security answers, and load it the same way.

**Verification:** Grep the repository for 'PRIVATE KEY' and confirm there is no match. Then confirm that a token signed with the old key is rejected with 401 after rotation.

**Reference:** [CWE-321: Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html)

---

<a id="m-179"></a>
#### M-179 — Pin RS256 in every JWT verification call

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — JWT verification does not pin the signing algorithm
- 🟡 [F-084](#f-084) — JWT verification accepts token-chosen algorithm

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` to `expressJwt` in `isAuthorized` and `denyAll`.
2. Replace `jws.verify(token, publicKey)` with `jws.verify(token, 'RS256', publicKey)` or `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })`.
3. Add `algorithms: ['RS256']` to the `jwt.verify` call in `updateAuthenticatedUsers`.
4. Upgrade `express-jwt`, jsonwebtoken and jws to versions that reject alg none.
5. Pass { `algorithms: ['RS256']` } to `jwt.verify` in `routes/verify.ts` and to every other verification call.
6. Configure `express-jwt` and jws verification with an explicit `RS256` algorithm.
7. Upgrade jsonwebtoken to a version that rejects tokens whose algorithm is not allowed.

**Verification:** Send a token with alg none, and one `HS256`-signed with the public key, to `/rest/2fa/status` and `/rest/order-history/orders`. Both must return 401/403.

---

<a id="m-222"></a>
#### M-222 — Strip privileged fields from user registration

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-075](#f-075) — User registration accepts privileged role from request body
- 🟠 [F-076](#f-076) — Self-registration accepts a client-supplied admin role

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:408`

**How:**

1. Add a pre-create hook or middleware on POST `/api/Users` that deletes role, `deluxeToken`, `totpSecret`, `isActive` and id from `req.body`, or builds the model from an explicit allow-list (email, password, username).
2. Force role to 'customer' server-side on registration and grant other roles only through an admin-only path.
3. Add middleware before the finale create on POST `/api/Users` that deletes role, `deluxeToken`, `isActive`, `totpSecret` and id from `req.body`.
4. Force role to 'customer' server-side, for example in a `create.write.before` hook on the User resource.
5. Allow admin role assignment only through an endpoint guarded by an admin role check.

**Verification:** Run: curl -X POST `/api/Users` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"email":"t@t.t","password":"x","passwordRepeat":"x","role":"admin"}'. Assert that the returned `data.role` is 'customer'.

---

<a id="m-223"></a>
#### M-223 — Bind security answer creation to the authenticated user

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-077](#f-077) — Unauthenticated security answer creation for arbitrary user IDs

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

1. Require `isAuthorized` and `appendUserId` on POST `/api/SecurityAnswers`, or create answers only inside the registration handler.
2. Ignore any client-supplied UserId and take it from the verified token.

**Verification:** curl -X POST `http://localhost:3000/api/SecurityAnswers` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"UserId":999,"SecurityQuestionId":1,"answer":"x"}' without a token returns 401.

---

### P2 — This Sprint

<a id="m-167"></a>
#### M-167 — Scope basket query to authenticated user

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — Missing object ownership checks on basket, basket item, checkout and review

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:426`

**How:**

1. In `retrieveBasket` (`routes/basket.ts`), query BasketModel with where: { id, UserId: `req.body.UserId` }, using the verified user id from `appendUserId`.
2. In `placeOrder` (`routes/order.ts`), change the lookup to `BasketModel.findOne({ where: { id, UserId: req.body.UserId } })`.
3. Reject a basket that does not belong to the caller before any order is placed or item is deleted. The parts disagree on the status code: the basket retrieval part proposes 404 and the checkout part proposes 403.
4. Add an ownership predicate that ties the basket item to the caller in the PUT `/api/BasketItems/:id` handler.
5. In `routes/updateProductReviews.ts`, add author: `user.data.email` to the update selector and return 403 when user is undefined.
6. Return 404 when no review matches the caller's ownership filter.

**Verification:** Log in as user A and try each endpoint against objects owned by user B: GET `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's basket id>, POST `/rest/basket/`&lt;B's basket id>`/checkout`, PUT `/api/BasketItems/`&lt;B's item id>, and PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with B's review _id. Every request should be rejected with 403 or 404, or the review update should report modified as 0. B's basket items and review message should stay unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

---

<a id="m-173"></a>
#### M-173 — Verify JWT signature before trusting chat user identity

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-013](#f-013) — Chat identity taken from JWT decoded without signature

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.decode` with `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })` or use `security.authenticatedUsers.from(req)`.
2. Reject tool calls needing identity when verification fails.

**Verification:** Send `/rest/chat` with an alg none token claiming another user's id, ask for that user's order, and assert `getOrderById` returns 'Customer not authenticated'.

**Reference:** [CWE-347: Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html)

---

<a id="m-174"></a>
#### M-174 — Require and verify the current password on change

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Password change skips the current-password check

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-620](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/620.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Reject the request when current is missing or empty, and always compare it with the stored hash.
2. Move the endpoint to POST with a JSON body so that passwords do not appear in URLs or logs.
3. Invalidate existing sessions after a successful password change.

**Verification:** Call `/rest/user/change-password` with a valid token and with new and repeat but no current parameter. It must return 401 and leave the stored password unchanged.

---

<a id="m-175"></a>
#### M-175 — Validate token audience server-side before OAuth login

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — OAuth login trusts userinfo profile without checking

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-345](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/345.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:28`

**How:**

1. Move the token handling to the server and verify the token via the tokeninfo endpoint or an ID token signature check.
2. Reject the token unless aud/azp equals the configured `client_id`, then derive the identity from the verified sub claim.

**Verification:** Submit an access token issued for another `client_id`. Confirm login is rejected and that a token issued for this `client_id` still works.

---

<a id="m-176"></a>
#### M-176 — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (F-017)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Google sign-in uses implicit flow without state

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:148`

**How:**

1. Replace `response_type=token` with `response_type=code` using a PKCE `code_verifier` and `code_challenge`, and exchange the code server-side.
2. Generate a random state value (and a nonce for OIDC), store it in `sessionStorage`, and reject callbacks whose state does not match.
3. Send access tokens in an `Authorization` header, not in the URL query string.

**Verification:** Confirm the authorization URL contains state and `code_challenge`. Then open the app at /#`access_token`=**** (8 chars) with no prior authorization request, and also replay a callback with a tampered state. Confirm the client rejects both without calling userinfo or login.

---

<a id="m-177"></a>
#### M-177 — Replace derived OAuth passwords with server-verified ID tokens

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — OAuth account password derived from the user's email

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. Remove the client-side `btoa(reversed email)` password and stop creating OAuth accounts with a usable local password. Use no password or a server-generated random secret that the client never sees.
2. Send the Google ID token (authorization code flow with PKCE) to a server endpoint that verifies signature, aud, iss and exp before it issues a session.
3. Flag OAuth-only accounts and reject password login for them.
4. Rotate the passwords of existing OAuth accounts.

**Verification:** For an OAuth-registered user, POST `/rest/user/login` with the email and `btoa(reversed email)` and confirm the response is 401.

**Reference:** [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

---

<a id="m-180"></a>
#### M-180 — Pin RS256 in all JWT verification calls

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — JWT verification does not pin the expected signing

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:55`

**How:**

1. Replace `jws.verify`/decode with `jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })` and use its returned payload.
2. Configure `express-jwt` with `algorithms: ['RS256']` in `isAuthorized()`.
3. Upgrade jws/express-jwt to versions that require an explicit algorithm.

**Verification:** Submit a token with header alg 'none' and another `HS256`-signed with the public key to `/rest/order-history/orders` and `/rest/2fa/verify`; both must be rejected.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: JSON Web Token For Java Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-181"></a>
#### M-181 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (F-025)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — JWT verify call missing algorithms allowlist

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:189`

**How:** Always pass `algorithms: ['RS256']` (or the project's chosen alg) as the third argument's options object to `jwt.verify`.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-005 scanner check; it reports no match at `lib/insecurity.ts:189`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-182"></a>
#### M-182 — Require emailed one-time reset token

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — Unauthenticated password reset relies only on a security answer

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-013](#w-013)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) - Weak Password Recovery Mechanism

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**How:**

1. Replace the security-question reset with a single-use, time-limited token sent to the registered email.
2. Key rate limits for `/rest/user/reset-password` on the account and on a trusted proxy-derived IP rather than the client-controlled X-Forwarded-For header.
3. Lock the account after repeated failed reset attempts.
4. Notify the user of reset attempts.
5. Stop returning the full updated user object in the reset response.

**Verification:** Send 101 reset requests with wrong answers for one account while rotating X-Forwarded-For values, and confirm the requests over the limit are rejected with 429. Then confirm a reset cannot complete without the emailed token.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Forgot Password Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Forgot_Password_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-183"></a>
#### M-183 — Commit lockfiles and install from them

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — Dependency lockfile generation disabled by npm configuration

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-051](#w-051)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `.npmrc:1`

**How:**

1. Remove the `package-lock=false` setting from `.npmrc`, then generate and commit `package-lock.json` for both the root and frontend packages.
2. Replace npm install with npm ci (plus `--ignore-scripts` where possible) in CI, release and `Dockerfile` builds so installs fail on lockfile drift.
3. Add a minimum release age or cooldown and automated dependency update tooling, so lockfile changes are reviewed in pull requests.

**Verification:** Run `grep -c 'package-lock=false' .npmrc` and confirm it returns 0. Confirm `git ls-files package-lock.json frontend/package-lock.json` lists both files. Confirm the CI logs show `npm ci` succeeding against the committed lockfile.

**Reference:** [docs.npmjs.com: Package Lock Json](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v10/configuring-npm/package-lock-json)

<a id="m-184"></a>
#### M-184 — Validate amount and settle payment before crediting wallet

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-028](#f-028) — Wallet top-up amount accepted from client

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/wallet.ts:27`

**How:**

1. Validate balance as a positive number within a configured maximum, and reject strings, negatives and non-finite values.
2. Credit the wallet only after a payment authorization for that amount succeeds, and record the transaction.

**Verification:** Send PUT `/rest/wallet/balance` with `balance=-5`, balance='abc' and `balance=1e9`, and confirm each returns 400 and leaves the wallet unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-20: Improper Input Validation](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)

<a id="m-188"></a>
#### M-188 — Use typed query predicates instead of \$where

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Route id interpolated into MarsDB \$where predicates

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. In `showProductReviews`, parse the id as an integer and reject NaN.
2. In `showProductReviews`, query with { product: id } instead of a \$where string.
3. In `trackOrder`, always strip the id to [\w-] or validate it against the `orderId` format.
4. In `trackOrder`, query with { `orderId: id` } instead of \$where.

**Verification:** Request GET `/rest/products/sleep`(2000)`/reviews` and confirm it returns 400 immediately with no 2-second delay. Then request GET "`/rest/track-order/`'%20||%20true%20||%20'" and confirm at most one order, or none, is returned.

<a id="m-189"></a>
#### M-189 — Require scalar string review id and single update

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-035](#f-035) — NoSQL operator injection in review update and like selectors

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**How:**

1. In both handlers, reject the request with 400 unless typeof `req.body.id` === 'string'.
2. Use the validated string id in every selector of each handler.
3. Remove `multi: true` from the review update so that one request updates at most one document.
4. Add author: `user.data.email` to the update selector.

**Verification:** Send PATCH `/rest/products/reviews` with `{"id":{"$ne":-1},"message":"x"}` and confirm a 400 response with zero modified reviews. Send POST `/rest/products/reviews` with `{"id":{"$ne":null}}` and confirm a 400 response with `likesCount` unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-943: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements in Data Query Logic (NoSQL Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html)

<a id="m-190"></a>
#### M-190 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (F-036)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-036](#f-036) — Username spliced into Pug template source before
- 🟠 [F-060](#f-060) — Stored username evaluated as JavaScript on profile

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-019](#w-019), [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)
- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**How:**

1. Keep the Pug template static and pass username as a local, for example `fn({ username, ... })`, rendered with escaped #{} interpolation.
2. Compile the template once at startup instead of per request.
3. Reject control characters and newlines in usernames on update.
4. Delete the eval branch in the profile handler and treat username purely as data.
5. Validate usernames on update against a strict character allow-list.

**Verification:** Set the username to a#{7*7} and load `/profile`, and confirm that the literal text renders rather than 49. Then set a username containing a newline followed by '- var x = 1' and confirm that it renders literally.

**Reference:** [CWE-1336: Improper Neutralization of Special Elements Used in a Template Engine (Server-Side Template Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html)

<a id="m-191"></a>
#### M-191 — Remove sanitizer bypass and render comments as text

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Stored XSS

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

**How:**

1. Remove the `bypassSecurityTrust` calls for feedback comments in the about and administration components.
2. Keep the comment out of HTML template strings: build the about-page caption with Angular template bindings, render the comment with {{ }} interpolation, and keep only the static star markup as trusted HTML.
3. Sanitize feedback comments on the server with a maintained HTML sanitizer before storage, as defense in depth.

**Verification:** POST feedback with the comment &lt;img `src=x` `onerror=alert`(1)&gt;, then load #`/about` and #`/administration` and confirm no script runs and the payload shows as text.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-197"></a>
#### M-197 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (F-043)

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — User passwords stored as unsalted MD5 hashes without a strength policy

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-050](#w-050)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `models/user.ts:76`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.hash` in the User password setter with Argon2id, bcrypt (cost of at least 12) or scrypt, which store a per-user salt and a tuned work factor.
2. Update login, and 2FA setup and disable, to verify passwords with the library's constant-time verify or compare function instead of comparing `hash()` output.
3. Migrate existing `MD5` hashes by rehashing each password on that user's next successful login, or force a password reset.
4. Add a model validation or a service check that enforces a minimum length of 12 or more and rejects breached passwords at registration, password change and reset.

**Verification:** Create two users with the same password and read the stored password column. The test must fail if a value matches /`^[a-f0-9]{32}$`/ or equals `md5(password)`, and pass only if both values have a \$argon2id\$ or \$2b\$ prefix and differ from each other. Then try to create a user with password 'a' and expect a validation error.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Password Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/changePassword.ts:39`

**How:**

1. Replace `security.hash` in the password setter with argon2id, or bcrypt at cost 12 or higher, with a per-user salt.
2. Use the library's constant-time verify function in login and change-password.
3. Rehash legacy `MD5` passwords on the next successful login and force a reset for the rest.

**Verification:** Register a user and check that the stored password starts with \$argon2id\$ or \$2b\$. Login and change-password must still succeed with the correct password.

---

<a id="m-206"></a>
#### M-206 — Validate and allowlist outbound request targets

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-054](#f-054) — Server-side request forgery

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) - Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/profileImageUrlUpload.ts:24`

**How:**

1. Allow only https URLs, resolve the host, and reject private, loopback and link-local addresses.
2. Set redirect: 'manual' and re-check every redirect hop, or fetch through an egress proxy.
3. Validate the response content type and magic bytes as an image before storing it.
4. Set a fetch timeout and a maximum body size.

**Verification:** POST `imageUrl`=`http://127.0.0.1:3000/metrics` and `imageUrl`=`http://127.0.0.1:3000/rest/admin/application-configuration` to `/profile/image/url`. Confirm both are rejected and no file is written.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Server Side Request Forgery Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Server_Side_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-207"></a>
#### M-207 — Query orders by equality selector instead of \$where

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-055](#f-055) — NoSQL \$where injection in order tracking

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Replace the \$where string with `ordersCollection.find({ orderId: String(id) })`.
2. Always apply the [^\w-] strip or a strict `orderId` format check regardless of challenge flags.

**Verification:** GET `/rest/track-order/`'%20||%20true%20||%20' and confirm at most a placeholder result with no other orders.

**Reference:** [OWASP: 05.6 Testing For Nosql Injection](https://owasp.org/www-project-web-security-testing-guide/latest/4-Web_Application_Security_Testing/07-Input_Validation_Testing/05.6-Testing_for_NoSQL_Injection)

<a id="m-209"></a>
#### M-209 — Return a status only from password reset

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-057](#f-057) — Password reset response leaks TOTP secret

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:46`

**How:**

1. Replace `res.json({ user: updatedUser })` with a fixed success message.
2. Never serialize `totpSecret`, password or `deluxeToken` in any user response.

**Verification:** After a successful POST `/rest/user/reset-password`, the response JSON has no `totpSecret` or password field.

#### M-210 — Restrict user listing to admins and allowlist fields

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-058](#f-058) — Authentication details route exposes all user records to any

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-213](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/213.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:601`

**How:**

1. Add an admin role check before `retrieveUserList`.
2. Map each user to an explicit allowlist (id, email, `lastLoginTime`) instead of spreading `dataValues`.

<a id="m-211"></a>
#### M-211 — Enforce coupon eligibility and cap in tool code

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-059](#f-059) — Model-selected coupon tool enforces discount policy only

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-20](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/20.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Constrain the schema with `z.number().int().min(1).max(10)` and re-check the bound inside execute.
2. Require a verified authenticated user and a server-verified damaged order before execute issues a coupon; record issuance per order to prevent reuse.

**Verification:** Send a chat whose model output calls `generateCoupon` with discount 50 and assert the tool returns an error and no coupon; unit-test `execute({discount: 50})` is rejected.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM062025 Excessive Agency](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm062025-excessive-agency/)

<a id="m-212"></a>
#### M-212 — Enforce server-side authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-061](#f-061) — Any authenticated user can create catalog products

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-285](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/285.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:369`

**How:**

1. Add a role check middleware (admin) to POST `/api/Products`, and restore and harden the PUT `/api/Products/:id` guard.
2. Default-deny the generated write endpoints of catalog models.

**Verification:** Send POST `/api/Products` with a customer token and confirm HTTP 403. Send PUT `/api/Products/1` without a token and confirm HTTP 401.

<a id="m-213"></a>
#### M-213 — Bind user record reads to the authenticated caller

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-062](#f-062) — Any authenticated user can read any user record by id

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:365`

**How:**

1. Add middleware on GET `/api/Users/:id` that compares `req.params.id` with the authenticated user's id and returns 403 unless they match or the caller is an admin.
2. Add `deluxeToken` and `lastLoginIp` to the `excludeAttributes` of the User resource.

**Verification:** Using a customer token, GET `/api/Users/`&lt;other id&gt; must return 403, and GET `/api/Users/`&lt;own id&gt; must not contain `deluxeToken`.

<a id="m-214"></a>
#### M-214 — Bind recycle lookups to the authenticated user

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-063](#f-063) — Unauthenticated read of any recycle record by parsed ID

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:388`

**How:**

1. Register `security.isAuthorized()` and `appendUserId()` before `getRecycleItem` on GET `/api/Recycles/:id`.
2. Add UserId: `req.body.UserId` to the where clause so that only the caller's records are returned.
3. Replace `JSON.parse(req.params.id)` with a strict scalar integer parse.

**Verification:** Send GET `/api/Recycles/[1,2]` without a token and confirm 401. Send GET `/api/Recycles/`&lt;other user's id> with user A's token and confirm an empty result or 404, while A's own records still return.

#### M-215 — Enforce successful payment before role upgrade

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-067](#f-067) — Deluxe membership granted without payment

- [CWE-841](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/841.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**How:**

1. Reject any `paymentMode` other than wallet or card.
2. Fail when the wallet is missing or balance is insufficient, and decrement atomically before updating the role.

**Verification:** POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode`=none; expect 400 and unchanged role.

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:485`

<a id="m-219"></a>
#### M-219 — Restrict product updates to an admin role

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-072](#f-072) — Unauthenticated callers can modify any product including its

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:501`

**How:**

2. Restrict the updatable product attributes to an allow-list in a finale `update.write.before` hook.

**Verification:** curl -X PUT `http://localhost:3000/api/Products/1` -H '`Content-Type`: application/json' -d '{"price":0.01}' must return 401 or 403, and GET `/api/Products/1` must still show the original price.

<a id="m-220"></a>
#### M-220 — Enforce coupon limits and eligibility in tool code

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-073](#f-073) — Chatbot generateCoupon tool issues arbitrary discounts

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. In `generateCoupon.execute`, reject discount values outside 1..10 with `z.number().int().min(1).max(10)` and an explicit server-side check.
2. Require an authenticated caller in `generateCoupon.execute` and refuse unauthenticated calls.
3. Require an `orderId` argument and verify server-side that the authenticated user owns a damaged or verified order before issuing a coupon.
4. Record issued coupons per user.
5. Gate coupon issuance behind human or privileged approval, or a server-side workflow, instead of the model's choice.

**Verification:** Call the `generateCoupon` tool's execute with discount 50, both directly and through a chat request that makes the model call it, and assert that it returns an error and no coupon code is streamed. Call it without an authenticated user and assert that it is refused.

**Reference:** [OWASP GenAI: LLM062025 Excessive Agency](https://genai.owasp.org/llmrisk/llm062025-excessive-agency/)

---

<a id="m-221"></a>
#### M-221 — Reject deluxe upgrades without a successful payment

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-074](#f-074) — Deluxe role granted without verified payment

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-863](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/863.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/deluxe.ts:43`

**How:**

1. Allow-list `paymentMode` to wallet or card and return 400 otherwise.
2. Upgrade the role only after a payment branch has succeeded.

**Verification:** Automated test: POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` as a customer with `paymentMode` 'none' and assert HTTP 400 and unchanged role.

<a id="m-224"></a>
#### M-224 — Enforce coupon policy server-side

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-078](#f-078) — Prompt injection makes chatbot issue coupons with arbitrary

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:184`

**How:**

1. Bound discount in the schema and in execute (`z.number().int().min(1).max(10)`) and reject values outside the bound on the server.
2. Require an authenticated, verified user and look up a damaged, owned order before issuing a coupon; do not rely on prompt text.
3. Accept only user-role messages from the client and rebuild prior assistant and tool turns from server-side state.

**Verification:** Run an integration test that calls the `generateCoupon` tool's execute with discount 50 and asserts it is rejected. POST `/rest/chat` with an injection payload and assert that no `couponCode` with discount > 10 is returned.

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-005](#w-005)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

2. Allow-list the updatable address fields, and never accept UserId or id from the body.

**Verification:** As user A, send PUT `/api/Addresss/`&lt;id of user B's address>. Expect 404 or 403, and confirm that B's address row is unchanged.

<a id="m-244"></a>
#### M-244 — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-102](#f-102) — Third-party GitHub Actions referenced by mutable tag or branch

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-051](#w-051)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `.github/workflows/image_actions.yml:33`

2. Configure Dependabot or Renovate for github-actions so the SHA pins stay updated.

**Verification:** Run grep -`rnE` 'uses: [`^@]+@(v[0-9]+|main|master)\s*$`' .github/workflows and confirm it returns no matches.

<a id="m-148"></a>
#### M-148 — Sign container images and verify signatures before use

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-099](#f-099) — Missing Container Image Signing

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`

**How:**

1. Add a keyless cosign sign step (or actions/attest-build-provenance) to the release workflow that signs the pushed image by digest, with `id-token: write` granted only to that job.
2. Publish the signature and provenance attestation alongside the image in the registry.
3. Document and enforce signature verification (cosign verify with the expected workflow identity and issuer) in deployment and consumer instructions.

**Verification:** Run cosign verify against the released image digest and confirm it succeeds with the expected workflow identity. Confirm an unsigned or tampered image fails verification, and that the normal release still publishes.

<a id="m-149"></a>
#### M-149 — Install with npm ci --ignore-scripts in Dockerfile builds

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-101](#f-101) — Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-027](#w-027)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `Dockerfile:4`

**How:**

1. Change the `Dockerfile` dependency install to npm ci `--ignore-scripts` so dependency lifecycle scripts do not run during the build (requires a committed lockfile).
2. Explicitly run only the required build or rebuild steps for packages that genuinely need a postinstall, and review each one.
3. Set `ignore-scripts=true` in the CI environment or `.npmrc` where compatible.

**Verification:** Build the image and confirm no dependency postinstall output appears in the logs. Confirm the application still builds and starts, and that any required native modules work.
